8. Domain Model

8.1 Entity Taxonomy

Entity Crate Cardinality Lifecycle
Plane vc-core 3 per Frame (Y, U, V) allocated at frame creation; freed on DPB eviction
Frame vc-core 1 per coded picture input → encoded → reconstructed → stored in DPB → evicted
Macroblock vc-core W/16 × H/16 per Frame scratch during encode/decode; not persisted
Partition vc-core 1–16 per MB (mode-dependent) sub-MB inter unit; lifetime == MB
TransformBlock vc-core 1–16 per MB per plane scratch; replaced after dequant
NalUnit vc-bitstream 1–many per coded picture serialized byte slice; freed after parse/write
SliceHeader vc-bitstream 1–many per coded picture parsed once, referenced during MB decode
Dpb vc-encoder/decoder 1 per codec session owns reference Frames; persists for GOP duration
RefList vc-inter 2 per P/B-slice (list0, list1) rebuilt per slice from DPB
BitWriter/BitReader vc-bitstream 1 per encode/decode session owns the RBSP byte buffer

8.2 Frame State Machine

State transitions (encoder path):
  RAW_INPUT --> COLOR_CONVERT --> MACROBLOCK_PARTITION --> MODE_DECISION
  MODE_DECISION --> INTRA_PREDICT | INTER_PREDICT
  INTRA_PREDICT | INTER_PREDICT --> RESIDUAL_COMPUTE --> TRANSFORM --> QUANTIZE
  QUANTIZE --> ENTROPY_CODE --> BITSTREAM_EMIT
  QUANTIZE --> DEQUANTIZE --> INV_TRANSFORM --> RECONSTRUCT --> DEBLOCK --> DPB_STORE

State transitions (decoder path):
  BITSTREAM_IN --> NAL_PARSE --> SLICE_PARSE --> MB_PARSE
  MB_PARSE --> ENTROPY_DECODE --> DEQUANTIZE --> INV_TRANSFORM
  INV_TRANSFORM --> INTRA_PREDICT | INTER_COMPENSATE
  INTRA_PREDICT | INTER_COMPENSATE --> RECONSTRUCT --> DEBLOCK --> DPB_STORE --> OUTPUT

Encoder and decoder share DEQUANTIZE → INV_TRANSFORM → RECONSTRUCT → DEBLOCK → DPB_STORE (the reconstruction loop). Any divergence between these paths violates I2.

8.3 Frame Type and GOP Semantics

FrameType state within GOP:
  IDR (I) → P → P → B → B → P → B → B → P → ... (configurable cadence)

Constraints:
  - IDR resets DPB; no frame after IDR may reference across it.
  - B-frames reference frames with lower AND higher POC (bi-directional, I4).
  - POC (Picture Order Count) governs display order; frame_num governs decode order.
  - DPB size limited to max_ref_frames (default 4); eviction by marking (short/long-term).
FrameType Ref lists DPB sources MB modes allowed
I none none IntraNxN, Intra16x16
P list0 (past) 1–4 decoded frames Intra*, Inter*, Skip
B list0 (past) + list1 (future) 1–4 each direction Intra*, Inter*, BiPred, Skip

8.4 Macroblock Type Model

EBNF for macroblock syntax tokens:

macroblock     ::= mb_type mb_data
mb_type        ::= 'I_NxN' | 'I_16x16_<mode>' | 'P_L0_<part>' | 'B_<pred>_<part>' | 'P_Skip' | 'B_Skip'
mb_data        ::= intra_data | inter_data
intra_data     ::= (intra4x4_pred_mode* cbp) | intra16x16_data
inter_data     ::= (ref_idx_l0? mv_l0?)* (ref_idx_l1? mv_l1?)* cbp residual?
cbp            ::= coded_block_pattern  -- 6 bits: 4 luma 8x8, 2 chroma
residual       ::= luma_residual chroma_residual
MbType Partitions Reference lists Residual
IntraNxN 16 × 4x4 blocks none 16 × 4x4 DCT + DC Hadamard
Intra16x16(mode) 1 × 16x16 none 1 Hadamard DC + 16 × 4x4 AC
Inter(16x16) 1 list0 [+ list1 for B] 1 × 16x16 (4 × 8x8 sub-blocks)
Inter(16x8) 2 × 16x8 per-partition 2 × 8x8 sub-partitions
Inter(8x16) 2 × 8x16 per-partition 2 × 8x8 sub-partitions
Inter(8x8) 4 × 8x8 per-partition 4 × 4x4 sub-blocks each
Skip 1 inferred (MV=0 or predicted) none (CBP=0)

8.5 Intra Prediction Modes

4x4 Luma (9 modes)

Mode ID Name Prediction direction
0 Vertical top samples extrapolated down
1 Horizontal left samples extrapolated right
2 DC mean of available top + left
3 Diagonal_Down_Left 45° toward bottom-left
4 Diagonal_Down_Right 45° toward bottom-right
5 Vertical_Right ~26.6° from vertical toward right
6 Horizontal_Down ~26.6° from horizontal toward bottom
7 Vertical_Left ~26.6° from vertical toward left
8 Horizontal_Up ~26.6° from horizontal toward top

16x16 Luma / 8x8 Chroma (4 modes each)

Mode Prediction
DC (0) mean of all available border samples
Horizontal (1) replicate left column
Vertical (2) replicate top row
Plane (3) linear plane fit through border (H.264 sec. 8.3.3.4)

Availability rule (I4): a neighbor sample is available iff its macroblock has been decoded (scanning order: left, top, top-left, top-right). At frame/slice boundaries, unavailable samples are substituted with 128 (midgray for 8-bit).

8.6 Motion Vector Model

MotionVector { x: i16, y: i16 }  -- quarter-pel units
  integer-pel offset = (x / 4, y / 4)
  fractional part    = (x % 4, y % 4), values 0|1|2|3 → full|quarter|half|three-quarter

MV range: [-512, 511] quarter-pel = [-128, 127.75] luma pixels (conformant constraint)
Chroma MV: scaled x/2 and y/2 in chroma quarter-pel units (one chroma pel = 2 luma pel)

MV prediction (median of A, B, C neighbors):

A = left MB/partition MV
B = above MB/partition MV
C = above-right MB/partition MV (above-left if C unavailable)
MVP = (median(A.x, B.x, C.x), median(A.y, B.y, C.y))
MVD = MV_coded - MVP  -- transmitted; decoded: MV_decoded = MVP + MVD

Sub-pixel interpolation stages:

Fractional Luma filter Chroma filter
Integer direct copy direct copy
Half-pel 6-tap [ 1,-5,20,20,-5,1 ] / 32 bilinear 1/2
Quarter-pel bilinear average of adj. integer/half bilinear 1/4

8.7 Integer DCT and Quantization Layout

H.264 4x4 integer forward DCT (matrix form, scaled):

Cf = Hf * C * Hf^T  (applied to 4x4 residual block C)
Hf = [[ 1, 1, 1, 1],
      [ 2, 1,-1,-2],
      [ 1,-1,-1, 1],
      [ 1,-2, 2,-1]]
Post-multiply by scaling matrix Ef element-wise, then >> 1 to get Cf.

Quantization (forward):

level = (|coeff| * MF[qp%6][i][j] + f) >> (15 + qp/6)
  MF[qp%6][i][j] — per-position multiply factor from H.264 Table 8-15
  f = (qp < 24) ? (1<<(qp/6+15))/3 : (1<<(qp/6+15))/6  -- dead-zone
sign(level) = sign(coeff)

Dequantization:

coeff_dq = level * V[qp%6][i][j] << (qp/6)   (if qp/6 >= 2)
         = level * V[qp%6][i][j] >> (2 - qp/6) (otherwise)
  V[qp%6] — per-position rescale table

QP ladder: step doubles every 6 QP increments. Chroma QP = chroma_qp_table[luma_qp] (capped at 39 for standard profiles).

Hadamard DC block (16x16 intra luma): 4x4 Hadamard over the 16 DC coefficients from 4x4 sub-blocks before main quantization step.

8.8 CAVLC Encoding Layout

Context per 4x4 block: nC (number of coefficients, derived from left/top neighbors).

Encoding order per 4x4 block:
  1. coeff_token(totalCoeff, trailingOnes) — VLC from Table 9-5 indexed by nC
  2. trailing_ones_sign_flag[i]            -- 1 bit each, most-recent first
  3. levels[i]                             -- suffix_length-coded, remaining non-zero coeffs
  4. total_zeros                           -- VLC from Table 9-7
  5. run_before[i]                         -- VLC from Table 9-10, scanning reverse zigzag

Zigzag scan order (4x4):

 0  1  5  6
 2  4  7 12
 3  8 11 13
 9 10 14 15

CAVLC roundtrip invariant (I6): for any sequence of integer coefficients in range [−512, 511], cavlc_decode(cavlc_encode(coeffs)) == coeffs.

8.9 Simplified CABAC Model

Binary arithmetic coder state:

CabacState { range: u16, low: u32, context_models: [CtxModel; N_CTX] }
CtxModel   { pStateIdx: u8, valMps: u1 }  -- 64-state LPS probability table

Encode step (one bin). qCodIRangeIdx = (range >> 6) & 3 selects rLPS = rangeTabLPS[pStateIdx][qCodIRangeIdx] from the state table, and range is reduced by rLPS. On the LPS branch, low += range and range is replaced by rLPS, then the LPS state-transition table updates pStateIdx/valMps; on the MPS branch only the MPS state-transition table updates pStateIdx. Both branches renormalize afterward, shifting range left (and emitting bits with carry propagation) until range >= 256.

Binarization schemes used:

Syntax element Scheme
mb_type truncated unary + suffix
MVD exp-Golomb order 3
coded_block_flag FL (1 bit)
coeff_abs_level_minus1 unary + EG0
end-of-slice FL (1 bit)

CABAC context count: ~460 contexts (simplified implementation may use subset).

8.10 NAL Unit and Bitstream Wire Format

NAL stream layout (Annex B byte stream):
  start_code := 0x000001 | 0x00000001 (4-byte for first, 3-byte otherwise)
  nal_unit   := forbidden_zero_bit(1) nal_ref_idc(2) nal_unit_type(5) rbsp_bytes

nal_unit_type values (relevant subset):
  1 = coded_slice_non_IDR
  2 = coded_slice_data_partition_A
  5 = coded_slice_IDR
  6 = SEI
  7 = SPS (sequence parameter set)
  8 = PPS (picture parameter set)

Emulation prevention: pattern 0x000001 or 0x000002 within RBSP replaced by
  0x000003 0x00 / 0x000003 0x01 / 0x000003 0x02 during write;
  0x000003 byte removed during parse. Applies inside RBSP, not start codes.

RBSP trailing bits: append 1-bit, then 0-bits to byte boundary.

Exp-Golomb code (ue):

x       code
0     → 1
1     → 010
2     → 011
3     → 00100
k     → [log2(k+1)] zeros + (k+1) in binary

Signed exp-Golomb (se): map k → (k > 0) ? 2k-1 : -2k before ue encoding.

8.11 DPB and Reference List Semantics

Dpb { frames: Vec<DpbEntry>, max_size: usize }
DpbEntry { frame: Frame, frame_num: u32, poc: i32, ref_type: ShortTerm | LongTerm | Unused }

Reference list construction (simplified, P-slice):
  list0 = sort(short_term frames, descending poc) ++ sort(long_term, ascending long_term_idx)
  list0 truncated to num_ref_idx_l0_active

B-slice: list1 = frames with poc > current_poc (ascending) ++ frames with poc <= current_poc (descending)
  list0 = frames with poc < current_poc (descending) ++ frames with poc >= current_poc (ascending)

Marking (MMCO):
  end_of_sequence → mark all unused
  mark_short_as_long_term → promote DpbEntry
  adaptive_memory_control_command → MMCO sequence in slice header
  sliding_window (default) → evict oldest short-term when DPB full

Invariant I8: the RefList presented to motion compensation must exactly match what a conformant decoder would construct from the same slice header and DPB state.

8.12 Deblocking Filter Model

Boundary strength (BS) decision table:

Condition BS
MB boundary; either side intra 4
MB boundary; neither intra 3
Sub-MB boundary; coded coefficients on either side 2
Sub-MB boundary; MV diff ≥ 1 integer pel or different ref frame 1
Otherwise 0

Filter application (BS ≥ 1). Thresholds α = α_table[clip3(0,51, QP_avg + α_offset)] and β = β_table[clip3(0,51, QP_avg + β_offset)] are looked up per edge. For each 4-sample edge [p2,p1,p0 | q0,q1,q2], the filter applies only when |p0-q0| < α AND |p1-p0| < β AND |q1-q0| < β; when that condition holds, BS=4 (intra boundary) selects the strong 5-tap filter and BS=1..3 selects the normal 3-tap or 4-tap filter (chosen by the dp, dq thresholds).

Chroma deblocking: same BS, separate α/β from chroma QP, 2-tap filter only.

Invariant I9: filter is applied after reconstruction, before DPB storage, in both encoder and decoder identically. Applying it only to display output (not reference) violates I2.

8.13 Reconstruction Loop Data Flow (Sequence Diagram)

Encoder                              Decoder (must mirror)
  source_block
      |
      v
  prediction = intra/MC(DPB)          prediction = intra/MC(DPB)
      |                                    |
  residual = source - prediction          (MV / mode from bitstream)
      |
  T = fwd_dct(residual)
  Tq = quantize(T, QP)
      |--- entropy encode --> bitstream --> entropy decode ---|
                                                              |
                                                         Tq (decoded)
      |                                                       |
  Tq_dq = dequantize(Tq, QP)                    Tq_dq = dequantize(Tq,QP)
  residual_rec = inv_dct(Tq_dq)                 residual_rec = inv_dct(Tq_dq)
  recon = clip(prediction + residual_rec)        recon = clip(prediction + residual_rec)
  deblock(recon) --> DPB                         deblock(recon) --> DPB

Encoder and decoder paths are identical from dequantize onward. The encoder runs both paths; the left branch feeds entropy coding, the right branch feeds the DPB.

8.14 Worked Example: Intra 4x4 Block Encode

Input residual block (after subtracting DC-predicted neighbors):

R = [[ 10, -3,  2,  0],
     [ -2,  5, -1,  1],
     [  0,  2,  3, -2],
     [ -1,  0,  1,  4]]
  1. Forward DCT → Cf (integer butterfly, exact).
  2. Quantize Cf at QP=28: MF[28%6=4], f dead-zone applied → Tq.
  3. CAVLC encode Tq in zigzag order: e.g., totalCoeff=5, trailingOnes=2, emit coeff_token + signs + levels + total_zeros + run_before.
  4. Decoder: parse → Tq recovered exactly (I6). Dequantize → Cf_dq (within step of Cf, I5). Inverse DCT → R_rec (within rounding of R, I3).
  5. recon = clip(prediction + R_rec, 0, 255).

Reconstruction error bounded by QP=28 step size ≈ 6 luma values per coefficient (I5).

8.15 Malformed / Failure-Path Examples

Malformed input Expected behavior
Missing emulation-prevention byte parse() returns Err(EmulationByte) before any state mutation
CAVLC totalCoeff > 16 Err(CavlcOverflow), slice parsing aborted
MV pointing outside reference frame + 8-pel border edge-extension clamps to border; no out-of-bounds read
B-frame referencing a frame not in DPB Err(DpbRefMissing), frame dropped
QP > 51 rejected at SPS/PPS parse; Err(QpOutOfRange)
Inverse DCT overflow (coefficients > defined range) saturate to i32 bounds, mark frame as corrupted
Slice header frame_num gap (lost frames) insert "gaps_in_frame_num_value_allowed" frames per H.264 sec. 8.2.5
End-of-stream mid-macroblock Err(UnexpectedEos), partial frame discarded

8.16 Edge Cases

Scenario Handling
Frame width or height not multiple of 16 pad to next multiple of 16 during encode; crop on display
First macroblock in first slice (no left/top neighbors) all unavailable → DC intra uses 128; inter MVP = (0,0)
Top-right neighbor unavailable at row end use top-left neighbor for 4x4 mode 4/5/7/8 availability
QP=0 (quantization step ~1) no overflow; level values fit i16; CAVLC VLC tables valid
All-zero residual block after quantization CBP bit = 0; no coefficients written; skip CAVLC for that block
IDR mid-sequence flush DPB; reset frame_num; reset POC counters
B-frame reordering (display ≠ decode order) POC used for reorder; DPB holds unreleased frames until display
Long-term reference promotion MMCO op in slice header; DpbEntry.ref_type updated
Chroma-only CBP set (luma all-zero) emit luma residual block count = 0; chroma blocks coded

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (stable toolchain) C/C++, Go, Zig Memory safety without GC; zero-cost abstractions; unsafe boundary isolates unsafe SIMD/bit-manipulation; H.264 integer transforms fit naturally in integer Rust C/C++ wins when integrating into an existing FFmpeg/x264 codebase or when mature hand-tuned assembly intrinsics dominate; Zig wins for fine-grained manual memory control with simpler C interop
ADR-02 Build system Cargo workspace (single Cargo.toml at root, per-crate members) CMake + cbindgen, Buck, Bazel Native Rust; workspace features for conditional compilation; reproducible lockfile; no external build orchestration needed at this scale CMake+cbindgen wins when shipping a C ABI library consumed by C/C++ projects; Bazel wins in a polyglot monorepo needing hermetic remote caching
ADR-03 Workspace structure 13 crates (vc-core … vc-cli) matching spine monolith single crate, 3-4 coarse crates Enforces P9 separation; each crate independently testable; compile times parallelized by Cargo A single crate wins for a tiny prototype where cross-crate API churn outweighs separation; coarse 3-4 crates win for a small team avoiding boundary bureaucracy
ADR-04 Dependency policy Minimal: clap (CLI), byteorder (bit I/O endianness), log/env_logger, criterion (bench), proptest (property tests), arbitrary + libfuzzer-sys (fuzz) heavy-dep approach (ffmpeg-sys, openh264-sys) Avoids GPL/patent encumbrance; keeps codec logic fully in-crate; no bindgen complexity ffmpeg-sys/openh264-sys win when you need production-grade, patent-licensed codecs immediately rather than an educational from-scratch implementation
ADR-05 Test vector / storage format Raw YUV4MPEG2 (.y4m) for input; raw .yuv 4:2:0 planar for unit fixtures; custom binary .vc NAL stream (Annex B byte stream) for bitstream output container formats (MP4/MKV), IVF No container parsing complexity; byte-exact comparison of decode output; standard tools (ffmpeg) can read y4m/yuv MP4/MKV/IVF win when interoperating with real players or streaming pipelines that expect a standard container with timing/muxing metadata
ADR-06 Serialization (bitstream) Hand-rolled BitWriter/BitReader + exp-Golomb (vc-bitstream) serde + bincode, protobuf, nom NAL/RBSP syntax requires sub-byte bit packing; serde cannot express this; hand-rolled is auditable against H.264 spec serde+bincode wins for byte-aligned sidecar/application metadata; it does not apply to sub-byte NAL syntax but does for an out-of-band stats file
ADR-07 Floating-point policy Forbidden in transform/quant/prediction hot paths; f32/f64 permitted only in PSNR metric computation and rate-control heuristics float DCT, float motion vectors P1/P3: bit-exactness requires integer-only transforms; float results are platform-dependent Float DCT wins for offline quality research where cross-platform bit-exactness is irrelevant and float SIMD throughput matters
ADR-08 Concurrency model Single-threaded encode/decode by default; frame-level parallelism via rayon (optional feature flag, not in Phase 1–13) async/tokio, thread-per-macroblock Frame-level dependencies (DPB, intra borders) require sequential MB order within a frame; rayon parallelism across independent frames is safe Thread-per-MB / wavefront (WPP) parallelism wins for HEVC/AV1-scale encoders where intra-frame parallelism is essential to hit real-time at 4K
ADR-09 Async model None (sync codec pipeline); async only in CLI I/O if needed tokio runtime throughout Codec is CPU-bound, not I/O-bound; async adds overhead without benefit async/tokio wins when the codec is embedded in a network streaming server where socket I/O multiplexing dominates the workload
ADR-10 SIMD / intrinsics Optional #[cfg(target_feature)] modules behind a simd feature flag; fallback pure-Rust always present mandatory SIMD (breaks portability), no SIMD ever Phase 1–13 target correctness; SIMD is Phase 14 optimization; pure-Rust fallback ensures CI portability Mandatory SIMD wins for a production encoder targeting fixed hardware where portability is irrelevant and every cycle counts
ADR-11 Integer transform implementation H.264 integer butterfly (Malvar et al. 2003 TCSVT) exact tables; fixed-point with defined shift counts float DCT, AAN algorithm, lookup-table DCT Normatively specified; bit-exact across platforms; matches reference H.264 behavior Float/AAN DCT wins for a JPEG-style still-image codec where the transform is not normatively integer-specified
ADR-12 Entropy coding choice CAVLC primary (Phase 7); simplified CABAC secondary (Phase 13) Huffman-only, range coder (HEVC-style), arithmetic-only H.264 specifies both; CAVLC simpler to implement and debug first; CABAC gives higher compression; both must roundtrip (I6) An HEVC/AV1-style range coder wins when targeting those standards' compression efficiency rather than H.264 conformance
ADR-13 Motion search algorithm Diamond search + EPZS as fast path; full exhaustive search as reference/verification path hierarchical search only, PDE optical flow Diamond/EPZS matches H.264 encoder practice (x264 lineage); full search validates fast path output; SATD cost for accurate half-pel prediction Hierarchical/pyramid search wins for large-motion 4K content where coarse-to-fine search escapes local minima diamond search falls into
ADR-14 Sub-pixel interpolation H.264-normative 6-tap luma half-pel filter + bilinear quarter-pel; H.264 chroma 1/8-pel bilinear bicubic (HEVC), nearest-neighbor Normatively specified; required for conformant motion compensation (P4) Bicubic / 8-tap (HEVC) interpolation wins when implementing HEVC or AV1, where the longer filters are normative
ADR-15 Rate-distortion mode decision Lambda * bits + SSE/SATD distortion; lambda from QP (sqrt formula); Lagrangian RD throughput-first greedy, VBSME only P5: correct RD tradeoff; lambda from QP matches H.264 standard practice Throughput-first greedy mode decision wins for real-time low-latency encoding (video calls) where RD-optimal search blows the latency budget
ADR-16 Rate control Frame-level CBR approximation: QP step from (actual_bits / target_bits) ratio; no complex buffer model in Phase 13 two-pass VBR, no rate control, full CPB buffer model Sufficient for the educational scope; complex rate control is out-of-scope Phase 14+ Two-pass VBR wins for offline file encoding where quality-per-bit is paramount; a full CPB buffer model wins for hardware-decoder (HRD) compliance
ADR-17 Logging log facade + env_logger; per-frame log at TRACE; per-MB at DEBUG disabled by default tracing/tokio-tracing, custom ring buffer Lightweight; standard ecosystem; structured per-frame output sufficient for P10 observability tokio-tracing wins when the codec runs inside an async service needing span-based distributed tracing
ADR-18 Metrics / observability Per-frame: bits, PSNR (Y/U/V/avg), QP, mode histogram, MB-type distribution; emitted as structured log lines (JSON key=value) at INFO level Prometheus metrics server, InfluxDB No service runtime; structured log lines parseable by standard tools (jq, awk); satisfies P10 A Prometheus endpoint wins when the encoder is a long-running transcoding service scraped by monitoring infrastructure
ADR-19 Testing framework cargo test (unit/integration); proptest (property-based for transforms, entropy, color); manual .yuv golden-file comparison for end-to-end pytest via FFI, custom test harness Standard Rust; proptest for I3/I6/I7 invariant testing; golden files for regression A pytest/FFI harness wins when QA engineers author conformance tests in Python against a C ABI
ADR-20 Fuzz testing cargo-fuzz + libfuzzer-sys; fuzz targets for vc-bitstream (NAL parse), vc-entropy (CAVLC decode), vc-decoder (full decode) AFL++ via ffi, honggfuzz Phase 14 hardening requirement; libfuzzer integrates with cargo fuzz; no unsafe needed in fuzz driver AFL++ wins when fuzzing the binary end-to-end with its advanced mutators, or fuzzing a non-libFuzzer target
ADR-21 Benchmarking criterion crate; per-phase micro-benchmarks (transform, quant, MB encode, motion search, deblock); end-to-end frame throughput (fps) custom timing harness, perf-stat scripts Statistical rigor; HTML reports; CI regression detection via criterion's threshold perf-stat / custom timing wins when profiling cache/branch hardware counters rather than statistical wall-clock benchmarking
ADR-22 CI/CD GitHub Actions: cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy -- -D warnings, cargo fmt --check, golden-file decode comparison, criterion bench (non-blocking) Jenkins, GitLab CI, local Makefile only Standard for Rust OSS; matrix over stable + beta Rust; clippy enforces code quality GitLab CI wins on self-hosted GitLab; Jenkins wins for complex on-prem matrix builds with custom hardware runners
ADR-23 Conformance testing Decoder output (raw YUV) compared byte-for-byte against known-good decode of in-house–generated bitstreams; PSNR >= 30 dB at QP=28 on standard sequences JM reference decoder comparison (would require JM license) Avoids GPL dependency; in-house generated bitstreams + known decode output provide bit-exact regression; JM comparison as manual spot-check JM reference-decoder comparison wins when formal H.264 conformance certification is required and the JM license is acceptable
ADR-24 Packaging / distribution cargo install from crate; single vc-cli binary; no C FFI ABI exposed in Phase 1–14 shared library (.so/.dll), WASM target CLI tool scope; C ABI is future work; WASM target conflicts with SIMD feature flag A shared library wins when embedding the codec into another application; a WASM target wins for in-browser decoding
ADR-25 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (Rust ecosystem dual-license) GPL-2 (x264/JM), LGPL Patent-free implementation; dual-license maximizes reuse; avoids viral GPL GPL wins when deriving directly from x264/JM source, where the viral license is unavoidable and acceptable
ADR-26 Reproducibility Cargo.lock committed; pinned toolchain via rust-toolchain.toml (stable channel, pinned version); no non-deterministic codegen flags no lockfile, nightly-only features Bit-exact CI: same inputs produce same output on any CI runner; required for golden-file testing Nightly-only features win when a critical portable_simd intrinsic is unstable and the perf gain justifies the reproducibility risk
ADR-27 Error handling thiserror-derived error enums per crate (e.g., BitstreamError, EntropyError); ? propagation; no panics in library code (only in test assertions) anyhow throughout, raw Box<dyn Error>, panics for codec errors Typed errors enable caller recovery (e.g., skip malformed frame); no panics in fuzz targets anyhow wins at the CLI binary boundary where ergonomic context chaining beats typed recovery
ADR-28 Memory model Owned Vec<u8> buffers in Plane; no Arc/Rc in hot paths; DPB owns Frame values; clone on reference-list construction arena allocator, GC, shared-ptr graph Ownership clarity; no aliasing in transform hot paths; DPB eviction is explicit Vec::remove; clone cost is one frame (bounded by max_ref_frames) An arena allocator wins for a massively parallel encoder where per-frame allocation churn dominates; Arc wins when frames are shared read-only across many parallel slice workers

11. Core Schema and Core Types

All types are defined in vc-core unless the owning crate is noted. These names are normative; all crates must use them without renaming.

11.1 vc-core — primitive types

Plane

pub struct Plane {
    pub data:   Vec<u8>,   // row-major samples, length == stride * height
    pub width:  u32,
    pub height: u32,
    pub stride: u32,       // bytes per row, >= width; may include padding
}
Property Value
Ownership owned; cloned when stored in Frame; borrowed as PlaneView/PlaneMut
Mutation mutable only through PlaneMut<'_>; Plane itself is !Sync while mutably borrowed
Concurrency Send (no interior mutability); multi-MB parallel processing borrows non-overlapping row slices
Persistence serialized as raw bytes (width × height samples, stride stripped) in YUV file I/O
Compatibility stride may grow in future (SIMD alignment); width/height fields are the authoritative extents 
pub struct PlaneView<'a>  { data: &'a [u8],     width: u32, height: u32, stride: u32 }
pub struct PlaneMut<'a>   { data: &'a mut [u8], width: u32, height: u32, stride: u32 }

Frame

pub struct Frame {
    pub planes:      [Plane; 3],   // [0]=Y luma, [1]=Cb chroma, [2]=Cr chroma (4:2:0)
    pub poc:         i32,           // picture order count; display order
    pub frame_type:  FrameType,
}
pub type FrameRef = Arc<Frame>;    // shared ownership in DPB reference lists
Property Value
Ownership Frame is owned by encoder/decoder per encode cycle; FrameRef = Arc<Frame> for DPB
Mutation after reconstruction is finalized, frame is frozen and wrapped in Arc; no in-place mutation after Arc creation
Concurrency Arc<Frame> is Send + Sync; readers do not lock
Persistence serialized as interleaved YUV 4:2:0 planar file (Y plane then Cb then Cr)
Compatibility poc may be extended to i64 if display range exceeds i32; planes index semantics are locked

FrameType

pub enum FrameType { I, P, B }
  • I — intra-coded; no inter-prediction; may be IDR (marks DPB flush when signaled in slice header)
  • P — uni-predicted from L0 reference list
  • B — bi-predicted from L0 and L1; may be used as reference (hierarchical-B)
  • Stored in Frame.frame_type; governs entropy context initialization, reference list construction

MbType

pub enum MbType {
    IntraNxN,                   // 4x4 luma partitions, each with an IntraMode
    Intra16x16(IntraMode),      // single 16x16 luma prediction mode
    Inter(PartShape),           // inter-coded with given partition shape
    Skip,                       // zero MV, zero residual; copied from spatial/temporal predictor
    PCM,                        // raw PCM escape (edge case; no transform/quant)
}

Macroblock

pub struct Macroblock {
    pub mb_type:   MbType,
    pub qp:        Qp,
    pub parts:     Vec<Partition>,      // 1..16 partitions depending on MbType
    pub residual:  Vec<TransformBlock>, // one per 4x4 luma block + chroma blocks
    pub cbp:       u8,                  // coded-block-pattern (which 8x8 sub-blocks have coeffs)
}
Property Value
Ownership owned inside encoder/decoder MB loop; not heap-shared
Mutation built incrementally during encode; frozen after entropy coding
Concurrency accessed single-threaded per slice; future: one Macroblock per thread if row-parallel
Persistence not persisted directly; reconstructed from bitstream
Compatibility parts length invariant: 1 for 16x16, 2 for 16x8/8x16, 4 for 8x8 (with sub-8x8 inside)

Partition

pub struct Partition {
    pub ref_idx: u8,            // index into RefList (L0 for P; L0/L1 for B)
    pub mv:      MotionVector,  // quarter-pel MV for this partition
    pub mv_l1:   Option<MotionVector>, // L1 MV for B-slice bi-prediction
}

MotionVector

pub struct MotionVector { pub x: i16, pub y: i16 }  // units: quarter-pel luma samples
  • Zero value ({0,0}) is the skip/direct predictor base
  • Range: [-8192, 8191] quarter-pel; validated at ME output, rejected by parser if out of range
  • Arithmetic: x + y = MotionVector (component-wise); no wrapping semantics

IntraMode

pub enum IntraMode {
    Dc, Vertical, Horizontal, DiagDownLeft, DiagDownRight,
    VertRight, HorizDown, VertLeft, HorizUp,  // 9 4x4 modes
    Plane,                                    // additional for 16x16 / chroma
}
  • Modes 0..8 match H.264 spec numbering; Plane is mode 3 in 16x16 context
  • IntraMode::default() = Dc (always available, even at frame edge)

TransformBlock

pub struct TransformBlock {
    pub coeffs: [i32; 16],   // 4x4 DCT coefficients; 64-element array for 8x8 blocks (future)
    pub is_8x8: bool,
}
  • Pre-quantization: values from integer forward DCT (bounded by source range × transform gain)
  • Post-quantization: values from vc-quant::forward_quant (dead-zone applied)
  • coeffs[0] is the DC coefficient; scan order is zigzag (H.264 4x4 scan, locked)

Qp

pub struct Qp(pub u8);   // invariant: 0 <= value <= 51
  • Constructed only via Qp::new(v: u8) -> Result<Qp, CodecError> (validates range)
  • Maps to quantization step via vc-quant::tables::qp_to_step(qp: Qp) -> u32
  • Chroma QP derived by vc-quant::chroma::chroma_qp(luma_qp: Qp) -> Qp (spec table)

PartShape

pub enum PartShape {
    P16x16, P16x8, P8x16,
    P8x8,                    // enables sub-MB partition (each 8x8 further split)
    P8x4, P4x8, P4x4,       // sub-MB partition shapes (only valid inside P8x8)
}

MvPredictor / MvDelta

pub struct MvPredictor { pub mv: MotionVector }  // median of A, B, C neighbors
pub struct MvDelta     { pub dx: i16, pub dy: i16 }  // signed difference vs predictor

11.2 vc-bitstream — I/O types

BitWriter / BitReader

pub struct BitWriter { /* internal: Vec<u8> buffer, bit offset */ }
pub struct BitReader<'a> { /* internal: &'a [u8], bit offset */ }

impl BitWriter {
    pub fn write_bit(&mut self, bit: u8);
    pub fn write_bits(&mut self, val: u32, n: u8);
    pub fn write_ue(&mut self, val: u32);          // exp-Golomb unsigned
    pub fn write_se(&mut self, val: i32);          // exp-Golomb signed
    pub fn into_bytes(self) -> Vec<u8>;
}
impl<'a> BitReader<'a> {
    pub fn read_bit(&mut self) -> Result<u8, CodecError>;
    pub fn read_bits(&mut self, n: u8) -> Result<u32, CodecError>;
    pub fn read_ue(&mut self) -> Result<u32, CodecError>;
    pub fn read_se(&mut self) -> Result<i32, CodecError>;
    pub fn bytes_remaining(&self) -> usize;
}
Property Value
Ownership BitWriter owns its buffer; BitReader borrows input slice
Mutation BitWriter is exclusively mutable; BitReader mutates bit position internally
Concurrency neither is Sync; one per encode/decode thread
Persistence output is RBSP bytes embedded in NAL units
Invariant I7 read_ue(write_ue(v)) == v; read_se(write_se(v)) == v

NalUnit

pub struct NalUnit {
    pub nal_type: NalType,
    pub rbsp:     Vec<u8>,   // RBSP payload after emulation-prevention unstuffing
}
pub enum NalType {
    NonIdrSlice = 1, IdrSlice = 5,
    Sei = 6, Sps = 7, Pps = 8,
    AudDelimiter = 9, EndOfSeq = 10, EndOfStream = 11,
}
  • Serialization: start code (0x000001) + NAL header byte + stuffed RBSP
  • Emulation prevention: insert 0x03 after any 0x000001 or 0x000002 in RBSP (I7)
  • NalUnit::parse(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<NalUnit, CodecError> — rejects malformed (fuzz target)

SliceHeader

pub struct SliceHeader {
    pub slice_type:  SliceType,   // I, P, B (mapped from H.264 slice_type values 0..9)
    pub frame_num:   u32,         // wraps at MaxFrameNum (SPS-derived power of two)
    pub poc:         i32,
    pub ref_list_l0: RefList,
    pub ref_list_l1: RefList,     // empty for I/P slices
    pub qp_delta:    i8,          // slice_qp_delta; base QP = PPS.qp + qp_delta
}
pub enum SliceType { I, P, B }

11.3 vc-inter — reference management types

RefList

pub struct RefList {
    pub frames: Vec<FrameRef>,   // ordered list of decoded reference pictures (L0 or L1)
}
  • Max length: 4 frames (§4.1 DPB reference frames default); validated at construction
  • Invariant I8: frames present in RefList are always in the DPB; stale entries are errors not silently skipped

Dpb

pub struct Dpb {
    pub frames:    Vec<FrameRef>,         // all decoded pictures currently held
    pub max_size:  usize,                 // capacity = min(sps.max_ref_frames, 4)
}
impl Dpb {
    pub fn insert(&mut self, frame: FrameRef, marking: PicMarking);
    pub fn evict_unused(&mut self);
    pub fn build_ref_list_l0(&self, cur_poc: i32) -> RefList;
    pub fn build_ref_list_l1(&self, cur_poc: i32) -> RefList;
    pub fn flush(&mut self);             // IDR: mark all as unused, drain
}
pub enum PicMarking { ShortTerm(u32), LongTerm(u32), Unused }
Property Value
Ownership Dpb owns Arc<Frame> references; encoder and decoder each own a Dpb
Mutation mutable only during reconstruction loop (encoder) and slice decode (decoder)
Concurrency not shared across threads; future: frame-parallel requires explicit lock
Invariant I8 build_ref_list_l0/l1 panic in debug if a frame is requested that is not in frames

11.4 vc-encoder — encode-side types

EncodeParams

pub struct EncodeParams {
    pub width:        u32,
    pub height:       u32,
    pub fps_num:      u32,
    pub fps_den:      u32,
    pub gop_size:     u32,       // IDR interval in frames
    pub b_frames:     u8,        // B-frames between consecutive P-frames (0 = no B)
    pub qp:           Option<Qp>, // constant QP if Some; rate control if None
    pub target_kbps:  Option<u32>,
    pub color_matrix: ColorMatrix,
}

GopConfig

pub struct GopConfig {
    pub idr_interval:     u32,
    pub b_cadence:        u8,        // e.g. 2 = IBBP pattern
    pub hierarchical_b:   bool,
}

RdCost

pub struct RdCost { pub distortion: u32, pub bits: u32, pub lambda: f32 }
// cost = distortion + lambda * bits; lambda fixed per QP (not persisted)
  • lambda is not serialized; recomputed from QP at encode time
  • Invariant I5 / P5: mode chosen with minimum RdCost.total() (= distortion + lambda*bits)

11.5 vc-core — error and result

CodecError

pub enum CodecError {
    InvalidQp(u8),
    BitstreamOverrun,
    EmulationPreventionError,
    ExpGolombOverflow,
    UnsupportedSyntax(&'static str),
    NalTypeMismatch { expected: NalType, got: NalType },
    DpbFull,
    InvalidMotionVector(i16, i16),
    InvariantViolation(&'static str),
}
pub type CodecResult<T> = Result<T, CodecError>;
  • InvalidQp, BitstreamOverrun, EmulationPreventionError are triggered from fuzz targets (Phase 14)
  • InvariantViolation used exclusively in debug_assert-equivalent paths for I1–I10

11.6 Type cross-reference table

Type Crate Used in Phases Key Invariants
Plane / PlaneView / PlaneMut vc-core 1–14 stride >= width; length == stride*height
Frame / FrameRef vc-core 1–14 planes[0]=Y 4:2:0; poc monotone in display order
FrameType vc-core 1, 6, 11 matches SliceType in slice header
Macroblock vc-core 5–14 parts.len() consistent with MbType
MbType vc-core 5–14 Skip implies zero CBP and zero residual
Partition vc-core 8–14 ref_idx < RefList.frames.len()
MotionVector vc-core 8–14 I4: only DPB frames; range validated
IntraMode vc-core 5–14 I4: only decoded neighbors used
TransformBlock vc-core 3–14 I3, I5: coeffs bounded post-quant
Qp vc-core 4–14 0..=51; only via Qp::new
NalUnit vc-bitstream 2–14 I7: emulation-prevention lossless
BitWriter/BitReader vc-bitstream 2–14 I7: ue/se roundtrip
SliceHeader vc-bitstream 2–14 I7: parse(serialize) idempotent
RefList vc-inter 9–14 I8: frames valid DPB entries
Dpb vc-inter 9–14 I2, I8: encoder == decoder state
EncodeParams vc-encoder 6–14 gop_size > 0; fps_den != 0
RdCost vc-encoder 13–14 P5: lambda >= 0; distortion >= 0
CodecError vc-core 1–14 no panics in release; errors surfaced

Phase 1 — core types + YUV color space

1.1 Objectives

  • Define the canonical type vocabulary in vc-core used by every downstream crate; no I/O, no algorithms.
  • Implement full RGB↔YUV BT.601 and BT.601/BT.709 conversion and 4:2:0 chroma subsampling in vc-color.
  • Establish raw YUV file read/write utilities for test vector injection and diffing.
  • Property-test that a roundtrip (RGB→YUV→RGB) is within rounding tolerance.
  • No float in core numeric types; conversion uses fixed-point (coefficient tables × 256 or 2^n).

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

vc-core — types (no dependencies)

Type Shape Notes
Plane { data: Vec<u8>, width: u32, height: u32, stride: u32 } stride ≥ width; allows row padding
Frame { planes: [Plane; 3], poc: i32, frame_type: FrameType } planes[0]=Y, [1]=Cb, [2]=Cr
FrameType enum { I, P, B } —
Macroblock { mb_type: MbType, qp: Qp, parts: Vec<Partition>, residual: Vec<TransformBlock>, cbp: u32 } cbp = coded block pattern
MbType enum { IntraNxN, Intra16x16(IntraMode), Inter(PartShape), Skip, PCM } —
Partition { ref_idx: u8, mv: MotionVector } per inter prediction unit
MotionVector { x: i16, y: i16 } quarter-pel units
IntraMode enum { Dc, Vert, Horiz, Plane, DiagDownLeft, DiagDownRight, VertRight, HorizDown, VertLeft, HorizUp } 4 for 16x16; 9 for 4x4
TransformBlock { coeffs: [i32; 16] } pre- and post-quant DCT coefficients
Qp (u8) 0..=51
NalUnit { nal_type: u8, rbsp: Vec<u8> } emulation-prevention applied on write
SliceHeader { slice_type: u8, frame_num: u32, poc: i32, qp: Qp, ref_list: RefList } —
RefList Vec<u32> ordered DPB POC indices for inter prediction
Dpb { frames: Vec<Frame>, max_size: usize } decoded picture buffer
PartShape enum { M16x16, M16x8, M8x16, M8x8, M8x4, M4x8, M4x4 } inter partition shapes
  • vc-core/src/lib.rs re-exports all types; vc-core/src/frame.rs, macroblock.rs, motion.rs, entropy_types.rs split by domain.
  • All types #[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]; Plane::new(width, height, stride) allocates zeroed data.
  • Frame::new_yuv420(width, height, poc, frame_type) allocates Y at full resolution, Cb/Cr at width/2 × height/2.

vc-color — conversion + subsampling

  • rgb_to_yuv_bt601(r,g,b) -> (y,u,v): integer arithmetic, coefficients from BT.601 scaled ×256, output clamped 0..255 (Y), 16..240 (Y studio), 16..240 (Cb/Cr).
  • yuv_to_rgb_bt601(y,u,v) -> (r,g,b): reverse coefficients, arithmetic right-shift, clamp 0..255.
  • downsample_420(y_plane, cb_plane, cr_plane) -> Frame: average 2×2 pixel blocks for chroma (simple box filter); produces planes at width/2 × height/2.
  • upsample_420(frame) -> (luma, cb_full, cr_full): bilinear upsampling for chroma for display; not used in encode loop.
  • read_yuv420_file(path, width, height) -> Vec<Frame>: read raw interleaved Y, Cb, Cr planes, return one Frame per (widthheight + width/2height/2*2) bytes.
  • write_yuv420_file(path, frames): inverse of above; used to export test vectors.
  • All arithmetic via i32 intermediates; no f32/f64 anywhere.
  • BT.709 variant: separate coefficient table constants; same API surface. Anchors: ADR-01 (Rust), ADR-28 (owned Vec<u8> memory model), P9 (stage separation across these types).

1.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p vc-core + cargo test -p vc-color pass.
  • vc-color CLI integration (via vc-cli stub) can read a raw YUV420 file, convert Y plane to luma stats, write back, and verify identity.
  • All types usable by importing vc_core::*.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
rgb_to_yuv then yuv_to_rgb error max absolute pixel error ≤ 2 (rounding budget)
Chroma plane dimensions exactly (width+1)/2 × (height+1)/2
Plane stride invariant stride >= width asserted in constructor
Property test iterations ≥ 10 000 random RGB triples, 100% pass
Raw file roundtrip read_yuv420(write_yuv420(frames)) == frames exact
Compilation zero warnings on stable Rust toolchain

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
tests/fixtures/color/bt601_table.tsv 256 known RGB→YUV pairs from H.264 spec tables exact u8 match
tests/fixtures/color/foreman_cif_30f.yuv 30 frames of CIF (352×288) raw YUV; canonical reference PSNR of Y > 60 dB on identity decode
tests/fixtures/color/black.yuv, white.yuv boundary luma/chroma values Y=16, Cb=Cr=128 for black; Y=235, Cb=Cr=128 for white (studio swing)
tests/fixtures/color/gradient_rgb.png + expected .yuv smooth gradient, tests no banding pixel-exact after conversion
Malformed: tests/fixtures/color/truncated.yuv file too short for declared dimensions Err(IoError) or Err(UnexpectedEof)

1.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Chroma plane off-by-one for odd dimensions integer division floors; odd-width frames lose one column assert stride*height == data.len() fails at odd sizes use (dim+1)/2 for subsampled dimensions
Studio vs. full-swing confusion BT.601 specifies 16–235 for Y, 16–240 for Cb/Cr; mixing with full-range 0–255 incorrect colors; black appears gray parameterize range at Frame level with a ColorRange flag
i32 overflow in coefficient multiply r * 77 + g * 150 + b * 29 can reach 255*256 = 65 280 — safe; but a copy using i16 overflows incorrect chroma values near saturated primaries use i32 explicitly, never i16 intermediates
Stride miscalculation propagates silently stride set equal to width but plane allocated with padding green lines or garbage columns at frame edges assert in Plane::new and add stride >= width invariant check
BT.601 vs BT.709 mixed in a test different coefficient tables; small systematic error PSNR around 45 dB instead of >60 dB tag each Frame with ColorPrimaries enum

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • rgb_to_yuv_bt601 called per-pixel in test vectors only; not hot in encode loop (encode operates on YUV input).
  • Plane::new uses vec![0u8; stride*height]; single allocation; no fragmentation.
  • read_yuv420_file reads full frame batch; buffer size = one frame, iterate; avoids peak memory for large files.
  • Chroma downsampling: row-major loop, access pattern sequential → L1-friendly; no transposed writes.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • Width or height = 0 → Plane::new must return empty data, not panic.
  • Width or height = 1 → chroma plane 1×1; verify subsampling arithmetic.
  • Non-multiple-of-2 dimensions → (dim+1)/2 rule; document in Plane rustdoc.
  • poc wraps: i32 allows negative and ≥2^31; downstream DPB logic must treat as signed.
  • stride > width (padded): iteration must skip padding bytes; all row loops use stride, not width.

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Plane stride/size mismatch discovered at encode time → garbled blocks in every frame; detectable by comparing encoded luma mean to expected range.
  • Wrong color matrix → systematic PSNR drop across all test vectors; detected by bt601_table.tsv fixture test.
  • Blast radius: confined to vc-color; downstream crates consume Frame and never re-derive YUV coefficients.
  • Recovery: fix coefficient constants, re-run cargo test; no persistent state.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric: log Frame dimensions and ColorRange at TRACE level on construction.
  • Test vectors committed to tests/fixtures/color/; reproducible via git lfs if large.
  • vc-color exposes color_primaries() and matrix_coefficients() accessors for downstream VUI injection.
  • No runtime configuration; color matrix is a compile-time or call-site parameter.

Phase 2 — bitstream I/O

2.1 Objectives

  • Build the complete bitstream serialization/deserialization substrate in vc-bitstream.
  • BitWriter: MSB-first bit-level write with byte accumulator; flush partial byte with zero-padding.
  • BitReader: MSB-first bit-level read with peeking; detect read past end.
  • Unsigned exp-Golomb (ue(v)): encode/decode non-negative integers; signed variant (se(v)) by zigzag mapping.
  • NAL unit framing: RBSP payload → emulation-prevention byte stuffing → NAL byte stream; inverse for parsing.
  • RBSP trailing bits: one 1 bit then zero-padding to byte boundary.
  • Property invariant: parse(write(x)) == x for all syntax elements.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

vc-bitstream/src/bits.rs

  • BitWriter: fields buf: Vec<u8>, acc: u8, acc_bits: u8; methods write_bit(u8), write_bits(val: u32, n: u8), write_byte(u8), flush() -> Vec<u8> (appends partial byte with zero padding).
  • BitReader: fields data: &[u8], byte_pos: usize, bit_pos: u8; methods read_bit() -> Result<u8>, read_bits(n: u8) -> Result<u32>, read_byte() -> Result<u8>, bits_remaining() -> usize.
  • Both types implement Debug; no Clone (reader holds a borrow).

vc-bitstream/src/golomb.rs

  • ue_encode(v: u32, w: &mut BitWriter): compute M = floor(log2(v+1)); write M zero bits, then (v+1) in M+1 bits.
  • ue_decode(r: &mut BitReader) -> Result<u32>: count leading zeros M; read M more bits; result = (1 << M | bits) - 1.
  • se_encode(v: i32, w: &mut BitWriter): zigzag → ue; v > 0: 2v-1, v <= 0: -2v.
  • se_decode(r: &mut BitReader) -> Result<i32>: ue_decode → un-zigzag.
  • Max code length: for v = 2^31 - 1, code is 63 bits; guard against stack overflow in loops.

vc-bitstream/src/emulation.rs

  • stuff_emulation_prevention(rbsp: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>: scan for 0x000000, 0x000001, 0x000002; insert 0x03 before the third byte; O(n) single pass.
  • remove_emulation_prevention(nal_data: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>: remove 0x03 from 0x000003xx sequences; reconstruct RBSP.
  • Both are exact inverses; tested by property test with random payloads.

vc-bitstream/src/nal.rs

  • NalUnit::wrap(nal_type: u8, rbsp: Vec<u8>) -> NalUnit: stores raw rbsp.
  • NalUnit::to_byte_stream(&self) -> Vec<u8>: prepend [0,0,0,1] start code, stuff emulation prevention, append.
  • NalUnit::parse(data: &[u8]) -> Result<(NalUnit, &[u8])>: find start code, extract next NAL boundary, remove stuffing.
  • parse_nal_stream(data: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<NalUnit>>: repeatedly call parse until exhausted.
  • NAL types used: 1 (non-IDR slice), 5 (IDR slice), 7 (SPS), 8 (PPS).

vc-bitstream/src/rbsp.rs

  • write_rbsp_trailing_bits(w: &mut BitWriter): write 1 bit, then zero bits until byte-aligned.
  • skip_rbsp_trailing_bits(r: &mut BitReader): read and discard trailing bits; assert leading 1. Anchors: P8 (bitstream conformance), I7 (roundtrip invariant), ADR-06 (hand-rolled BitWriter/BitReader).

2.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p vc-bitstream passes all unit and property tests.
  • vc-cli inspect subcommand can parse a NAL byte stream, enumerate NAL types and byte offsets.
  • All downstream crates import BitWriter, BitReader, ue_encode, ue_decode, se_encode, se_decode, NalUnit from vc-bitstream.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
ue roundtrip ue_decode(ue_encode(v)) == v for all v in 0..=65535; 100%
se roundtrip se_decode(se_encode(v)) == v for v in -32768..=32767; 100%
Emulation prevention roundtrip remove(stuff(x)) == x for 100 000 random byte sequences
NAL parse roundtrip parse(to_byte_stream(nal)) == nal for 10 000 random rbsp payloads
BitWriter/BitReader bit-level roundtrip write N random bits, read back same N bits: 100%
Compile zero warnings

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
tests/fixtures/bitstream/ue_pairs.tsv known (v, bitstring) pairs from H.264 spec Table 9-2 exact bit match
tests/fixtures/bitstream/se_pairs.tsv known (v, bitstring) pairs for signed Golomb exact match
tests/fixtures/bitstream/emulation_examples.bin payloads with 0x000001, 0x000002, 0x000003 stuffed = known bytes
tests/fixtures/bitstream/single_nal.bin one NAL with start code parse yields exact type + rbsp
Malformed: tests/fixtures/bitstream/truncated_nal.bin start code with no payload Err(UnexpectedEof)
Malformed: tests/fixtures/bitstream/no_start_code.bin random bytes without 0x000001 Err(NoStartCode)

2.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
ue_encode(0) produces 1 (single 1 bit) but naive log2 gives -infinity log2(0+1) = 0, M=0; correct unit test ue_encode(0) == [1b] use (v+1).leading_zeros() arithmetic, not float log
Emulation-prevention insertion shifts byte offsets NAL offsets computed before stuffing are invalid after slice header offset mismatch at parse always measure offsets in RBSP space, stuff only at NAL output
RBSP trailing bit in partial byte corrupts next field write_rbsp_trailing_bits called before all fields written decode desync after first NAL call trailing bits exactly once, as the last operation on a RBSP
read_bits(32) for 32-bit field reads 32 bits but u32 shift << 32 is UB in Rust debug shift by word size is defined as 0 in release but may panic in debug test failure only in debug builds implement read_bits as loop or special-case n=32
Start code 0x000001 inside NAL payload without emulation prevention codec emits raw ints that happen to contain start code sequence decoder finds spurious NAL boundaries always apply emulation prevention; unit test with 0x000001 in payload

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • BitWriter::write_bits inner loop per-bit is acceptable for metadata (SPS/PPS/headers); hot path for residuals uses write_bits(val, n) bulk form.
  • stuff_emulation_prevention scans linearly; on typical RBSP (KB range) is negligible; no SIMD needed at this phase.
  • parse_nal_stream allocates one Vec<u8> per NAL; total allocation proportional to frame count × slice size.
  • BitReader holds a &[u8] slice reference; zero copy for read path.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty RBSP (rbsp: vec![]) → to_byte_stream still prepends start code + NAL header; parse roundtrip must succeed.
  • NAL payload that is entirely 0x00 bytes → emulation prevention must insert 0x03 after every two consecutive zeros followed by 0x00/0x01/0x02.
  • ue_decode with all-zero bit input → infinite leading-zero loop; guard with bits_remaining() == 0 returning Err(Overflow).
  • write_bits(val, 0) → no-op; must not panic or corrupt acc_bits.
  • Adjacent start codes: [0,0,0,1,0,0,0,1] → two back-to-back NALs each with empty payload.

2.9 Failure Analysis

  • Emulation-prevention bug → decoder finds phantom NAL boundaries mid-slice → parse error on every P/B frame after Phase 10.
  • ue_encode off-by-one → all syntax fields off by one → decode outputs garbage; detectable by roundtrip unit test before integration.
  • Blast radius: vc-bitstream is foundational; a bug here breaks all later phases; this is why property tests must run in CI.
  • Recovery: all state is per-encode; re-encode from source after fix.

2.10 Operational Notes

  • vc-cli inspect --input foo.264 lists NAL units: index, type, byte offset, RBSP size.
  • Tracing: log NAL type and size at DEBUG level in to_byte_stream and parse.
  • No runtime state; no threading; no I/O beyond caller-supplied byte slices.
  • Benchmark: criterion bench for stuff_emulation_prevention on 1 MB payload; baseline target < 5 ms.

Phase 3 — integer DCT transform

3.1 Objectives

  • Implement the H.264 integer 4×4 forward and inverse DCT in vc-transform; no floating-point.
  • Implement the 8×8 integer DCT variant for completeness (used by High profile).
  • Implement the 4×4 Hadamard transform for the 16×16 luma DC array processing.
  • Guarantee I3: inverse_dct(forward_dct(block)) == block exactly at specified integer precision.
  • All operations on [i32; 16] (4×4) or [i32; 64] (8×8) arrays; no external crates for arithmetic.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

vc-transform/src/dct4x4.rs

H.264 integer 4×4 forward transform (from ITU-T H.264 sec. 8.5.12.1):

  • Horizontal pass: apply butterfly [a,b,c,d] -> [a+d, a-d, b+c, b-c] with scaled coefficients per the Cf matrix; exact integer.
  • Vertical pass: same butterfly applied column-wise to result of horizontal pass.
  • Normalization: forward transform includes factor-of-2 scaling absorbed into quantization step (Qp table in vc-quant); transform output is "pre-norm".
  • Signature: fn dct4x4_forward(block: &[i32; 16]) -> [i32; 16]
  • Signature: fn dct4x4_inverse(coeffs: &[i32; 16]) -> [i32; 16]
  • Inverse shift: right-shift by 6 (or 7 for 8-bit) after accumulation to return to pixel domain.
  • Scan order: coefficients in row-major order; zigzag scan applied by vc-entropy, not here.

vc-transform/src/dct8x8.rs

  • H.264 8×8 integer transform (High profile annex); same separable butterfly structure, 8×8 coefficient matrix.
  • Signature: fn dct8x8_forward(block: &[i32; 64]) -> [i32; 64]
  • Signature: fn dct8x8_inverse(coeffs: &[i32; 64]) -> [i32; 64]

vc-transform/src/hadamard.rs

  • 4×4 Hadamard for the 16 DC values of a 16×16 intra luma block (WHT).
  • Signature: fn hadamard4x4_forward(dc: &[i32; 16]) -> [i32; 16]
  • Signature: fn hadamard4x4_inverse(coeffs: &[i32; 16]) -> [i32; 16]
  • No scaling needed for Hadamard; all additions/subtractions; result divided by 2 at inverse.

vc-transform/src/lib.rs

  • Re-exports all six public functions.
  • TransformBlock wraps [i32; 16] or [i32; 64]; from vc-core.

Butterfly relation (4×4 forward, row operation, H.264 notation). For an input row [x0 x1 x2 x3], the four intermediate sums a = x0+x3, b = x1+x2, c = x1-x2, d = x0-x3 combine into the output row [a+b, 2c+d, a-b, c-2d] — an additions-and-shifts-only butterfly with no multiplies. The vertical pass applies the identical butterfly relation column-wise to the row-pass output.

Precision management:

  • Forward: inputs are residuals in range −255..255 (8-bit source); intermediate max ≈ 1020; fits i16 but use i32.
  • Inverse: coefficients after dequant in range determined by QP (see Phase 4); intermediate max fits i32.
  • Right-shift after inverse: arithmetic shift (>> 6); Rust >> on i32 is arithmetic — correct.
  • Bit-exactness depends on identical shift behavior on all platforms (guaranteed for i32 >> n in Rust). Anchors: P1/P3 (bit-exact, integer-only), I3 (roundtrip invariant), ADR-11 (integer butterfly), Malvar/Hallapuro/Karczewicz/Kerofsky (Required Reading Papers table).

3.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p vc-transform passes: roundtrip, known-vector, and overflow tests.
  • dct4x4_forward and dct4x4_inverse usable from vc-encoder and vc-decoder.
  • Hadamard forward/inverse verified against known 4×4 DC array.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
4×4 roundtrip: inverse(forward(block)) == block 100% for all blocks in −128..127 range (property test, 50 000 iterations)
8×8 roundtrip same, for −128..127 inputs
Hadamard roundtrip inverse(forward(dc)) == dc exact for 16 random i32 values in −255..255
Known-vector test dct4x4_forward on all-zeros block → all-zeros output
Flat block test dct4x4_forward([k; 16]) → DC coeff = 4k (or 8k depending on normalization); all AC = 0
No float grep -r "f32|f64" vc-transform/ returns empty
Compile zero warnings

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
tests/fixtures/transform/dct4x4_vectors.tsv 20 (input block, expected output coeffs) pairs from H.264 JM reference exact i32 match
tests/fixtures/transform/dct4x4_inverse_vectors.tsv known (coeffs → pixels) pairs exact match
tests/fixtures/transform/hadamard_vectors.tsv 5 DC arrays with expected Hadamard output exact match
tests/fixtures/transform/overflow_edge.tsv blocks at ±255 extremes verify no panic, output in valid range
Malformed: tests/fixtures/transform/wrong_size.bin 15 elements instead of 16 compile-time array type rejection; no runtime test needed

3.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Inverse shift amount off by one H.264 spec uses right-shift-6 but implementation uses 5 or 7 systematic ±2× DC error in reconstructed pixels validate against JM reference output for flat block
Forward butterfly sign error c-2d written as c+2d in one row AC coefficients have wrong sign; PSNR ~20 dB instead of >50 unit test against dct4x4_vectors.tsv catches immediately
Arithmetic shift vs logical shift i32 >> n is arithmetic in Rust; casting to u32 before shift makes it logical negative residuals rounded toward zero instead of toward minus infinity keep all intermediate types as i32; never cast to u32 before shift
Hadamard divide-by-2 in inverse omitted WHT inverse requires /2 at output DC coefficients 2× too large → bright reconstructed frames check hadamard_vectors.tsv fixture
Normalization mismatch between encoder and decoder forward includes scale factor that inverse does not cancel I3 violated; inverse(forward(x)) != x use the exact H.264 spec butterfly equations; validate via roundtrip property test
8×8 coefficient max overflow 8×8 input range ±255; intermediate sums can exceed 32-bit for naive scalar accumulation panic in debug (checked arithmetic) or silent wrap in release confirm max intermediate ≈ 255 × 8 × 8 × 8 = ~130 000 << 2^31 — safe in i32; document bound

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • 4×4 DCT called once per macroblock partition per encode cycle; at CIF 30fps = ~10 000 MBs/frame × 4 partitions = 40 000 calls/frame.
  • Separable 2-pass: 4 row butterflies + 4 column butterflies = 8 passes of 4 ops; total ~64 ops per block; fast even scalar.
  • Avoid heap allocation: all inputs/outputs are fixed-size arrays passed by reference.
  • SIMD (NEON/AVX2) deferred to Phase 14; Phase 3 targets correctness, not throughput.
  • Stack frames small (two [i32; 16] temporaries = 128 bytes); no risk of stack overflow.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • All-zero block → all-zero output; forward + inverse identity.
  • DC-only block (all values equal): AC coefficients must be exactly zero; verify numerically.
  • Block with ±127 residuals (maximum 8-bit residual): verify no overflow in i32 intermediates.
  • Block with ±255 values (for cases where prediction overshoot is clamped): extreme but valid.
  • Called with uninitialized residuals (programming error): Rust array init forces zeroing; safe.

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • I3 violated → encoder reconstructed frame diverges from decoder output → I2 violated → reference frame drift → error accumulation over GOP.
  • Detection: compare encoder reconstruction buffer to decoder output after each frame in Phase 6 integration test.
  • Blast radius: all phases that depend on prediction (Phases 6–13) produce incorrect output.
  • Containment: vc-transform is stateless; fix is a code patch and re-encode.
  • Postmortem: log intermediate butterfly values at TRACE level for first macroblock when drift detected.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • No runtime state; pure functions; thread-safe by construction.
  • Metric: count dct4x4_forward calls via an atomic counter behind a cfg(feature = "metrics") gate.
  • Benchmark: criterion microbench on dct4x4_forward for a 1080p-size batch; baseline target < 1 ns/block (scalar).
  • No logging in hot path; TRACE-level dump of block contents callable from integration test harness only.

Phase 4 — Quantization

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement vc-quant crate: forward quantization, dequantization, QP-to-step mapping.
  • Cover luma and chroma planes with distinct QP remapping.
  • Introduce dead-zone quantization as default (replaces rounding for coding efficiency).
  • Establish I5 (quant-dequant bound): |dequant(quant(x)) - x| ≤ step/2.
  • Provide QP ladder query: qp_to_mf(qp) → (MF, shift) and qp_to_qstep(qp) → f32.
  • No floating-point in hot path — multiply-shift only.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

File layout (vc-quant/src/): lib.rs (re-exports); tables.rs (quant_scale, dequant_scale, chroma_qp_map tables); forward.rs (quant_block(), quant_dc()); inverse.rs (dequant_block(), dequant_dc()); params.rs (QpParams, Qp newtype, qp_to_mf(), qp_to_qstep()).

Multiply-shift tables (H.264 sec. 8.5):

QP % 6 MF (4x4 luma) shift
0 13107 15+QP/6
1 11916 15+QP/6
2 10082 15+QP/6
3 9362 15+QP/6
4 8192 15+QP/6
5 7282 15+QP/6

quant(coeff, MF, shift, deadzone) → sign(c) * max(0, (|c|*MF + deadzone) >> shift)

Dead-zone: deadzone = (1 << shift) / 3 for intra (H.264 recommendation); / 6 for inter.

Dequantization:

dequant(level, QP) → level * V[QP%6] << (QP/6) where V is the 4x4 dequant scale table (H.264 Table 8-15).

Chroma QP mapping: chroma_qp = CHROMA_QP_TABLE[min(luma_qp, 51)] — clips at 51, maps via H.264 Table 8-16.

DC coefficients: separate forward/inverse paths using Hadamard-scaled quant tables; quant_dc divides by 2 extra, dequant_dc multiplies by 2 extra.

Interfaces (signatures only):

pub fn quant_block(coeffs: &mut [i32; 16], qp: Qp, intra: bool);
pub fn dequant_block(levels: &mut [i32; 16], qp: Qp);
pub fn quant_dc_2x2(coeffs: &mut [i32; 4], qp: Qp);
pub fn dequant_dc_2x2(levels: &mut [i32; 4], qp: Qp);
pub fn chroma_qp(luma_qp: Qp) -> Qp;
pub fn qp_to_qstep(qp: Qp) -> f32;   // display only, not in hot path

Property tests (proptest): for all QP in 0..=51 and random i16 inputs, verify |dequant(quant(x)) - x| ≤ qstep(qp)/2 + 1 (integer bounds).

CBP integration: quant_block returns bool (any non-zero level → sets CBP bit); Macroblock.cbp populated here. Anchors: I5 (quant/dequant bound), ADR-11 (integer arithmetic), Malvar/Hallapuro/Karczewicz/Kerofsky (Required Reading Papers table, quant-scale derivation).

4.3 Deliverable

  • vc-quant compiles, all unit tests pass.
  • Property tests for I5 pass across full QP range.
  • quant_block + dequant_block ready to integrate into Phase 6 encoder loop.
  • chroma_qp mapping confirmed against H.264 Table 8-16 reference values.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Requirement
I5 property test pass for all QP 0..51, 10 000 random i16 inputs
QP=0 identity dequant(quant(x, QP=0)) ≈ x within step
QP=51 extreme all non-DC coeffs → 0; DC survives
CBP flag all-zero block → CBP bit clear
Chroma QP table matches H.264 Table 8-16 exactly (51 reference pairs)
No float in hot path cargo clippy / grep confirms no f32/f64 in forward.rs/inverse.rs

Fixture Purpose Expected output

quant_qp27_luma.bin QP=27, typical mid-quality levels + reconstructed block

Malformed: QP=255 → Err(InvalidQp); empty block → no panic.

4.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Wrong shift for QP/6 group drift at QP multiples of 6 (QP 6,12,18..) off-by-one in shift = 15 + QP/6 compare to JM reference at those QPs
Luma table applied to chroma DC chroma DC over/under-quantized forgot chroma_qp() before DC path PSNR dip on chroma only
Dead-zone omitted slightly worse R-D vs reference using (1<<shift)/2 rounding instead CBP rate higher than expected
Integer overflow in MF multiply wraps on large AC coefficients DCT output can reach ~32767*16=524272; MF up to 13107 → needs i64 intermediate assertion on intermediate value
Dequant left-shift overflow i32 wraps at high QP for high level level * V << (QP/6) can exceed i32 at QP≥48 saturate to clip at ±(1<<15)
Chroma QP mapping applied twice excess chroma quality loss applying mapping in both vc-quant and caller log QP per component

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • quant_block runs once per 4x4 block per macroblock (16 luma + 8 chroma blocks): 24 calls per MB; all arithmetic fits in 32 registers, no heap allocation.
  • SIMD opportunity: 16-element i32 vector via std::simd or platform intrinsics (deferred to Phase 14); avoid branch per coefficient with a branchless sign * max(0, …) pattern.
  • Table lookups (6-entry MF table, 52-entry chroma map) fit in a cache line each.

4.8 Edge Cases

Edge case Handling
QP=0 step = minimum; no clamping needed; roundtrip within 1 LSB
QP=51 largest step; most coeffs → 0; DC may still be nonzero
All-zero input block; negative DCT coefficients CBP=0, skip entropy coding downstream; sign-magnitude split, dead-zone applies to magnitude
DC-only macroblock (CBP luma=0 except DC) only quant_dc called; quant_block skipped
Chroma QP clamp at 51 any luma QP > 39 maps chroma QP < luma QP

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Wrong MF table PSNR diverges from JM at specific QPs all frames at that QP replace with H.264 Table 8-13 values; re-run conformance
I5 property violation proptest finds counterexample reconstruction error unbounded bisect QP range; check shift computation
Overflow in dequant garbage pixel values, panic in debug entire frame corrupted add checked_shl + saturate; add assertion
Chroma table mismatch chroma PSNR wrong chroma planes only compare table byte-for-byte against spec

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Log per-frame average QP, per-block QP (if adaptive QP used), CBP distribution.
  • P10 (observability): expose QuantStats { avg_qp, zero_block_ratio, dc_only_ratio } per frame.
  • No migration concern — vc-quant is stateless; no stored state between frames.
  • At Phase 6 integration, quant_block output feeds directly into placeholder entropy; no interface change expected at Phase 7.

Phase 5 — Intra Prediction

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement vc-intra: all H.264 intra prediction modes for luma 4x4, luma 16x16, chroma 8x8.
  • Enforce P4 (causal prediction): predictors read only decoded, available neighbor samples.
  • Handle neighbor availability at frame edges, slice boundaries, and constrained-intra mode.
  • Provide predict_4x4(mode, neighbors) → [u8; 16] and predict_16x16(mode, neighbors) → [u8; 256].
  • Expose mode enumeration for mode-decision loop in Phase 6.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

File layout (vc-intra/src/): lib.rs; neighbors.rs (NeighborSamples, availability masks, edge extension); luma4x4.rs (9-mode 4x4 prediction); luma16x16.rs (4-mode 16x16 prediction); chroma8x8.rs (4-mode chroma prediction, DC/H/V/Plane); mode_check.rs (most-probable-mode / MPM computation, mode coding helpers).

4x4 luma modes (IntraMode enum):

Mode index Name Direction
0 Vertical top neighbors → downward
1 Horizontal left neighbors → rightward
2 DC mean of available top+left
3 Diagonal Down-Left extrapolates from top-right
4 Diagonal Down-Right 45° from top-left corner
5 Vertical-Right ~26° from vertical
6 Horizontal-Down ~26° from horizontal
7 Vertical-Left ~26° left of vertical
8 Horizontal-Up ~26° above horizontal

Each 4x4 mode: fills a [u8; 16] from a NeighborSamples struct (up to 4 top + 4 top-right + 4 left + 1 top-left).

16x16 luma modes:

Mode Logic
DC mean of top row and/or left column; fallback 128
Vertical replicate top row into all rows
Horizontal replicate left column into all columns
Plane linear fit H+V (Richardson sec. 7.3 formula): H = sum i*(p[top+i] - p[top-i-2]), V = similar left, a = 16*(p[15]+p[-15*stride]), scaled and clipped

Chroma 8x8 prediction (same 4 modes; operates on 8x8 U and 8x8 V independently):

  • DC: 4 sub-quadrant DCs from available neighbors.
  • Horizontal/Vertical: same structure as 16x16 but 8x8.
  • Plane: same formula scaled for 8x8.

NeighborSamples holds top: Option<[u8;4]> ([u8;16] for 16x16), top_right: Option<[u8;4]>, left: Option<[u8;4]>, top_left: Option<u8>. Availability rules: top unavailable at frame top edge; left unavailable at frame left edge; top-right unavailable for last MB in row (use top-right substitute = top[3] replicated per H.264 sec. 8.3.1.2).

Constrained intra prediction: if constrained_intra_pred_flag=1, disallow neighbors coded as inter; treat them as unavailable.

Most-probable mode (MPM): for each 4x4 block, derive MPM from left and top block modes; coded as prev_intra4x4_pred_mode_flag + rem_mode; exposed as mpm_of(left_mode: Option<IntraMode>, top_mode: Option<IntraMode>) -> IntraMode.

Interfaces: predict_intra4x4(mode: IntraMode4x4, nb: &NeighborSamples4x4) -> [u8; 16]; predict_intra16x16(mode: IntraMode16x16, nb: &NeighborSamples16x16) -> [u8; 256]; predict_chroma8x8(mode: IntraChromaMode, nb: &NeighborSamples8x8) -> [u8; 64]; mpm_of(left: Option<IntraMode4x4>, top: Option<IntraMode4x4>) -> IntraMode4x4; intra4x4_mode_sad(src: &[u8; 16], pred: &[u8; 16]) -> u32.

Tests per mode: compare against pre-computed reference arrays for all 9 4x4 modes (fixed neighbor values); verify DC fallback with both neighbors missing. Anchors: P4 (causal prediction — decoded neighbors only), I4 (neighbor-availability invariant), H.264 sec. 8.3 (Intra Prediction Process).

5.3 Deliverable

  • vc-intra compiles; all 9 × 4x4 modes, 4 × 16x16 modes, 4 × chroma modes tested.
  • Neighbor-unavailability paths exercised for each mode (top edge, left edge, corner MB).
  • MPM computation matches H.264 sec. 8.3.1.1 test vectors.
  • Ready to consume from Phase 6 encode loop (mode selection via SADcost).

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Requirement
4x4 mode unit tests 9 modes × 4 neighbor configs = 36 cases, all exact
16x16 DC fallback missing top+left → all 128; matches H.264 sec. 8.3.3
Plane overflow safety all samples clipped to [0, 255]; no panic on max inputs
Edge-MB availability top row MBs: top=unavailable; correct DC partial mean
Chroma DC 4-quadrant each quadrant uses correct neighbor subset

intra4x4_dc_no_neighbors.bin DC mode, no neighbors all 128
