Performance Engineering

Throughput Targets (tie to Phase 14 gate; §4.1 IDs in parentheses)

Codec path Resolution Target (software, 1 thread) Target (SIMD, 1 thread)
Encode I-frame 1080p 30 fps ≥ 15 fps ≥ 60 fps
Encode P-frame (M4/M5 floor) 720p 30 fps ≥ 20 fps ≥ 90 fps
Decode full GOP (M6/M7 floor) 1080p ≥ 120 fps ≥ 240 fps
Motion search (full) (M11) 16×16 block ≤ 500 µs ≤ 50 µs
CAVLC encode/decode (bounded by M8/M9 per-MB budget) MB residual ≤ 2 µs ≤ 0.5 µs

Benchmarking Strategy

  • Framework: criterion (stable statistical benchmarks with outlier detection).
  • Benchmark crates: one benches/ per crate; top-level vc-bench workspace member aggregates cross-crate scenarios.
  • Benchmark granularity:
    • Micro: single 4×4 DCT, single MB intra prediction, single CAVLC coeff encode.
    • Macro: full I-frame encode (1080p), full GOP encode (1080p, 30 frames), full 1080p decode.
    • End-to-end throughput: frames per second for encode and decode loops.
  • Input: synthetic YUV (flat, gradient, noise, natural CIF/1080p captured clips).
  • Criterion configuration: 100 samples, 5 s warm-up, measurement_time = 30s for macro benches.
  • Performance-regression CI: critcmp compares current vs. main-branch JSON; > 10% regression fails the gate.

Profiling Methodology

Tool Use Invocation
perf record + perf report Linux CPU hotspot perf record -g --call-graph=dwarf ./vc-cli encode ...
cargo flamegraph Flamegraph from perf cargo flamegraph -- encode -i input.yuv -o out.264
samply macOS / cross-platform samply record ./vc-cli ... → Firefox Profiler UI
heaptrack Heap allocation profiling heaptrack ./vc-cli encode ...; identify hot allocation paths
valgrind --tool=callgrind Instruction-accurate simulation for DCT / quant inner loops
DHAT (Rust dhat crate) In-process heap timeline enabled via --features dhat-heap

Flamegraph Workflow

  1. Build with RUSTFLAGS="-C force-frame-pointers=yes" + debug = true in release profile.
  2. Run cargo flamegraph on a 300-frame encode sequence.
  3. Identify dominant stacks: expected hotspots are vc-motion SAD inner loop, vc-transform DCT butterfly, vc-entropy CAVLC VLC table lookup.
  4. Profile again after SIMD optimization; compare flamegraphs side by side.
  5. Target: motion estimation ≤ 40% of encode wall time; transform+quant ≤ 20%; entropy ≤ 15%.

SIMD and Cache Analysis

Analysis Tool Target
L1/L2 cache miss rate perf stat -e cache-misses,cache-references < 2% L1 miss on DCT inner loop
Branch misprediction perf stat -e branch-misses < 1% in motion search hot path
SIMD utilization LLVM-MCA on generated asm verify AVX2 SAD uses _mm256_sad_epu8
Memory bandwidth likwid-perfctr DPB frame buffer reads < 50% of memory bandwidth budget at 1080p

Allocator Analysis

  • Default: system allocator; --features jemalloc for profiling.
  • DHAT integration: dhat::Heap guard in benchmark harness; assert no per-frame heap allocation in hot path after Phase 8 (pre-allocate DPB, MB residual buffers, motion search workspace).
  • Hot-path allocation budget: zero heap alloc per macroblock encode/decode cycle in steady state.

Tracing Methodology

  • tracing crate with structured spans at: frame encode start/end, MB mode decision, motion search, entropy coding slice.
  • tracing-chrome for timeline JSON → Chrome tracing UI.
  • Span attributes: frame_poc, mb_addr, mv_cost, rd_cost, mode.
  • Use case: diagnose uneven macroblock encode time (e.g., B-frame reference list overhead).

Tail-Latency Analysis

  • Criterion reports p50/p95/p99 per-frame encode latency.
  • Gate: p99 encode latency for a single 1080p frame ≤ 5× p50; outliers indicate DPB buffer stall or entropy coder reset overhead.
  • Track latency distribution across QP 18/28/38 to identify QP-dependent tails.

Memory Profiling

  • DPB size: bounded at max 16 reference frames × frame_size; assert DPB never exceeds this.
  • heaptrack check: total peak heap ≤ 4 × (luma + chroma) × max_ref_frames + encode workspace (≤ 600 MB for 1080p, 4 refs).
  • Residual buffer pool: pre-allocated arena per slice; confirmed zero per-MB alloc via DHAT.

Scalability Analysis

Dominant Scaling Pressures

Pressure Driver Ceiling
Frame resolution O(WH) work per frame (motion search, DCT, deblocking) Encode throughput scales linearly with pixel count; 4K is 4× 1080p
Reference frame count DPB size, ref-list construction, motion search over multiple refs O(R) per MB for multi-ref search; R ≤ 16 by spec
GOP length Drift error window; B-frame dependency depth Longer GOP → more P/B frames between IDRs → error concealment harder; seek latency grows
MB mode decision complexity O(modes × partitions) per MB Exhaustive 4×4 partition search × 9 intra + inter is the dominant single-thread bottleneck
Entropy coder state CABAC context tables per slice 460 context variables reset at each slice; slice count scales with resolution / slice-size settings

Horizontal Scaling (Thread-Level Parallelism)

Parallelism unit Feasibility Constraint
Slice-level parallel encode High — independent slices share no state Deblocking across slice boundaries disabled or restricted per spec
Tile-level decode High for decode; each tile independent Not in base H.264; reserved for future
Frame-level parallel encode Limited — B-frames require preceding I/P reconstruction Possible with frame-parallel decode (lookahead buffer)
MB-row parallel decode Moderate — wavefront parallel processing (WPP-style) Requires 1-row delay between threads; in-loop deblock complicates
SIMD within a core High — DCT, SAD, MC all vectorizable AVX2: 8 × u8 SAD in single instruction; target 4–8× scalar throughput

Vertical Scaling

  • Single-core performance governed by: SAD inner loop (memory-bound at 1080p), CAVLC VLC table lookup (cache-bound), deblocking filter (memory-bound boundary reads).
  • Adding CPU cores: near-linear speedup for slice-parallel decode up to ~8 threads; diminishing above 16 due to DPB synchronization (reference frame availability gating).
  • Memory bandwidth: at 1080p 30 fps with 4 reference frames, DPB reads ≈ 4 × 1080p × 30 = ~750 MB/s; exceeds L3 cache on most CPUs → main memory bandwidth is the scaling ceiling for multi-ref motion search.

Bottleneck Map

Resolution ↑         → motion search O(WH × search_range²) dominates
Reference frames ↑   → DPB bandwidth ceiling; memory-bound beyond 4 refs at 1080p
Thread count ↑       → slice-parallel encode saturates at ~8 threads (slice overhead)
GOP depth ↑          → B-frame RD complexity; seek latency grows O(GOP_len)
QP ↓ (high quality)  → CAVLC/CABAC output larger → I/O bandwidth + entropy coder time grows
Bitrate ↑            → larger residual blocks → transform+quant compute grows moderately

Scaling Ceilings

Dimension Current design ceiling Mitigation path
Resolution ~4K (limited by single-thread motion search) SIMD SAD + parallel MB-row search
Reference frames 4 refs before DPB bandwidth saturation at 1080p Reduce search range at high ref counts; hierarchical MV prediction
Threads ~8 slice-parallel effective threads Wavefront parallel decode; frame-parallel with lookahead
Bitrate (entropy) ~50 Mbit/s before CABAC becomes bottleneck CAVLC fallback for high-bitrate modes; parallel entropy segments
GOP length >250 frames causes seek latency > 8 s at 30 fps Insert periodic IDR frames; open-GOP with gradual decoder refresh

Coordination Overhead

  • DPB lock: single RwLock<Dpb> per sequence; read-contested by multi-ref motion compensation threads; mitigate with per-frame Arc<Frame> references (readers don't block each other).
  • Slice task queue: crossbeam work-stealing deque; encode slices posted as tasks; no shared mutable state between slice tasks except DPB reads.
  • Reconstruction loop barrier: all macroblock encodes in a frame must complete (and deblocking applied) before that frame enters the DPB; Arc<AtomicBool> per frame signals readiness.

Storage and Metadata Scaling

  • Bitstream size: O(WH / QP_quality_factor); PSNR-bitrate curves (RD curves) measured per sequence.
  • DPB memory: bounded by spec (max_num_ref_frames ≤ 16); implementation cap at 4 for Phase 11; increase to 16 in Phase 14 with explicit memory budget check.
  • Motion vector storage: 1 MV per 4×4 block per reference = O(WH / 16 × R) per frame; 1080p × 4 refs ≈ 518 K MVs ≈ 2 MB per frame; pre-allocated per-frame MV buffer, not heap-per-MB.
  • Conformance test corpus: ≈ 500 MB for full JVT conformance set; stored in tests/conformance/ with LFS; CI fetches on conformance step only.

Mental Models & First Principles

The compressed insights an expert video-codec engineer holds. Each predicts a bug class. Every cited § resolves to a real heading.

MM1. A codec is a feedback loop: the encoder must decode its own output. Prediction is formed from the reconstructed (lossy) frame the decoder will also have, not the original. Predicts: zero encoder/decoder drift across a long GOP (I2). Bug without it: predicting from the pristine source → reconstruction mismatch that compounds frame over frame. Applied: Phase 5–6 (reconstruction loop), §4.1 (0-bit drift / 300-frame GOP), I2.

MM2. Only quantization loses information. Prediction, transform, and entropy coding are all reversible; quantization is the single lossy step and the sole quality dial. Predicts: where to spend bits and why QP controls quality. Bug without it: hunting for quality loss in the entropy coder (there is none) instead of the quantizer. Applied: Phase 4 (transform/quant), §8 (domain model), MM6.

MM3. Motion estimation is a search; motion compensation is a copy. The search cost (SAD/SATD) is a proxy for bits, not for pixel error. Predicts: why SATD (frequency-domain) beats SAD for mode decision. Bug without it: minimizing raw pixel error and producing motion vectors that cost more bits than they save. Applied: Phase 8–9 (motion search/comp), ADR-13, MM10.

MM4. Integer transforms are normative; floating point drifts across platforms. The H.264 integer butterfly is bit-exact on every CPU; a float DCT is not. Predicts: cross-platform decode equality. Bug without it: a float transform decodes differently on x86 vs ARM, breaking conformance. Applied: Phase 4 (integer transform), ADR-07/ADR-11, P1/P3.

MM5. The DPB is a reference-counted cache with spec-defined eviction. Reference-picture management (sliding window / MMCO) decides which frames remain available for prediction. Predicts: correct reference selection and memory bound. Bug without it: evicting a frame still referenced → prediction from garbage → corruption. Applied: Phase 5–6 (DPB), §4.1 (DPB ≤ 16 refs), §11 Core Schema.

MM6. QP is a logarithmic quality dial: every +6 doubles the step size. Rate scales geometrically with QP, not linearly. Predicts: rate-control step behaviour. Bug without it: a linear QP→bits assumption makes rate control oscillate. Applied: Phase 12 (rate control), §4.1 (QP 0–51), ADR-16.

MM7. Entropy coding must roundtrip exactly — encode then decode is the identity. A single wrong VLC table entry desynchronizes the whole bitstream. Predicts: the I6 roundtrip invariant. Bug without it: an off-by-one in a CAVLC/CABAC table silently corrupts every subsequent symbol. Applied: Phase 7 (CAVLC), Phase 13 (CABAC), I6, ADR-12.

MM8. The in-loop deblocking filter is inside the reconstruction loop, so it is normative. Both encoder and decoder must apply identical filtering before the frame enters the DPB. Predicts: why an out-of-loop post-filter causes drift. Bug without it: filtering only on display (post-loop) → encoder and decoder DPBs diverge (MM1). Applied: Phase 10 (deblocking), I2, P3.

MM9. Decode order is a DAG, not a sequence: B-frames separate coding order from display order. Picture Order Count (display) and decode order differ when B-frames reference future frames. Predicts: correct frame reordering and output timing. Bug without it: emitting frames in decode order shows them out of sequence. Applied: Phase 9 (B-frames), §8 (POC), §11 Core Schema.

MM10. Rate-distortion is Lagrangian: minimize D + λ·R. λ, derived from QP, is the exchange rate between distortion and bits. Predicts: coherent mode decisions across block sizes. Bug without it: minimizing distortion alone yields a correct-looking but bloated bitstream. Applied: Phase 11 (RD mode decision), ADR-15, MM3.

Comparative Architecture Table

This design (H.264-baseline-style: integer transform + CAVLC/simple-CABAC + diamond/EPZS motion + in-loop deblock, single-threaded) versus four real codec implementations. Real systems only.

Dimension This blueprint (vc) x264 (H.264) x265 (HEVC) libvpx (VP9) SVT-AV1 (AV1)
Standard H.264 baseline+ subset H.264/AVC H.265/HEVC VP9 AV1
Transform 4×4/8×8 integer DCT 4×4/8×8 integer 4×4–32×32 4×4–32×32 DCT/ADST 4×4–64×64 DCT/ADST/IDTX
Entropy coder CAVLC + simple CABAC CAVLC + CABAC CABAC bool/arithmetic multi-symbol arithmetic
Block partition 16×16 MB → 4×4 16×16 MB 64×64 CTU quadtree 64×64 superblock 128×128 superblock
Motion search diamond/EPZS + full ref diamond/hex/UMH star/full + AMP diamond + NEWMV hierarchical + OBMC
Parallelism single-thread (rayon opt) frame/slice/sliced-threads WPP + frame tile + frame tile + segment + frame
RD optimization Lagrangian (λ from QP) Lagrangian RDO Lagrangian RDO RDO RDO + temporal filtering

x264 is the mature H.264 reference encoder with hand-tuned assembly across architectures and sophisticated psychovisual RDO; it wins for production H.264 at the best quality/speed tradeoff. This blueprint deliberately reimplements the H.264 baseline path in safe Rust for clarity and bit-exactness, deferring x264's assembly and advanced rate control.

x265 moves to HEVC's quadtree CTUs (up to 64×64) and mandatory CABAC, gaining ~50% compression at far higher encode cost; it wins when bandwidth savings justify compute. We target H.264's fixed 16×16 macroblocks because the quadtree and HEVC tooling are a different (larger) project (see Alternative Architectures and Deferred Features Register).

libvpx (VP9) is Google's royalty-free codec with superblocks and ANS/bool-coder entropy; it wins where patent-free licensing is the deciding constraint (web video). We implement H.264 for its pedagogical centrality and ubiquitous reference material, accepting H.264's patent status is moot for an educational from-scratch build (ADR-25).

SVT-AV1 is a scalable, heavily-parallel AV1 encoder built for many-core throughput with segment/tile parallelism; it wins for cloud transcoding at scale. We choose a single-threaded core (ADR-08) so the reconstruction loop and drift semantics (MM1) are observable without concurrency confounds; frame-parallelism is deferred.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

Numbers an expert computes before designing, each derived from §4.1. 8-bit 4:2:0 assumed (1.5 bytes/pixel).

  • C1 — Raw frame size (1080p). 1920 × 1080 × 1.5 = 3.11 MB/frame; the ≤ 6 MB/ref DPB budget (§4.1) leaves headroom for 10-bit samples or padded search borders.
  • C2 — DPB memory (4 refs, 1080p). 4 × 3.11 MB = 12.4 MB of reference frames resident; within the ≤ 128 MB working-memory budget (§4.1) alongside transform scratch and entropy tables.
  • C3 — Motion-vector storage (1080p, 4 refs). (1920/4)×(1080/4) × 4 refs × 4 B = 129 600 × 4 × 4 = 2.07 MB/frame (§4.1), pre-allocated per frame, not heap-per-MB.
  • C4 — Macroblocks per frame. 1080p = (1920/16)×(⌈1080/16⌉) = 120 × 68 = 8 160 MB; 720p = 80 × 45 = 3 600 MB — the unit over which all per-MB budgets (§4.1) accumulate.
  • C5 — Per-MB time budget (720p encode). ≥ 5 fps ⇒ 200 ms/frame ÷ 3 600 MB = ≈ 55 µs/MB average, well under the 5 ms p99 worst case (§4.1) — i.e. the worst MB may be ~90× the average.
  • C6 — Output bitrate (720p, QP 28). Raw 1280×720×1.5 = 1.38 MB/frame × 5 fps = 6.9 MB/s; at the ~20:1 ratio (§4.1) ⇒ ≈ 2.8 Mbps bitstream.
  • C7 — Decode/encode asymmetry. Decode target 30 fps vs encode 5 fps at 720p (§4.1) = 6× faster, because decode skips motion search (MM3) — it only copies via supplied vectors.
  • C8 — Hot-loop working set. One MB = 256 B luma + 2×64 B chroma = 384 B; a ±16 search window ≈ (16+32)² ≈ 2 304 luma samples/ref — the whole hot loop fits the ≤ 256 KB L2 budget (§4.1).
  • C9 — Transform operations per frame. Per MB: 16 luma + 8 chroma = 24 4×4 transforms; 1080p = 8 160 × 24 ≈ 196 K transforms/frame, the dominant integer-arithmetic load.
  • C10 — Bitstream buffer ceiling. ≤ 2× uncompressed (§4.1) bounds NAL byte-stuffing worst case: 1080p ⇒ ≤ 6.2 MB output buffer even on incompressible (QP 0) input.
  • C11 — Drift budget. 0 bits over a 300-frame GOP (§4.1): any nonzero reconstruction difference at frame N propagates to all N+1…300, so the tolerance is exactly zero (MM1).
  • C12 — Conformance corpus footprint. ≈ 500 MB JVT set (see Scalability Analysis) stored via LFS; fetched only on the conformance CI step, not every build.

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Each requires synthesizing ≥ 2 sections.

  1. Why must the encoder run a full decoder internally, and what fails over a long GOP if it does not? (MM1 + Phase 5–6 + §4.1 drift)
  2. Which single stage of the pipeline is lossy, and how does that shape where the encoder spends effort? (MM2 + Phase 4 + MM10)
  3. Why is SATD a better mode-decision cost than SAD despite costing more to compute? (MM3 + Phase 8 + ADR-13)
  4. Why are integer transforms normative, and what breaks if a float DCT is used? (MM4 + ADR-07/ADR-11 + §4.1)
  5. Trace one P-macroblock from motion search to reconstructed pixels, naming each crate and budget. (§3 + §4.1 + Phase 8–10)
  6. Why does evicting the wrong DPB frame corrupt future frames, and what governs eviction? (MM5 + Phase 6 + §11)
  7. How does QP relate to bitrate, and why does a linear rate-control model oscillate? (MM6 + Phase 12 + ADR-16)
  8. What guarantees CAVLC/CABAC encode→decode is the identity, and how is it tested? (MM7 + Phase 7/13 + I6)
  9. Why is the deblocking filter inside the reconstruction loop rather than a display post-filter? (MM8 + Phase 10 + MM1)
  10. How do decode order and display order diverge with B-frames, and what data structure tracks it? (MM9 + Phase 9 + §8 POC)
  11. Derive the per-macroblock time budget for 720p at 5 fps and explain the gap to the p99 target. (Capacity C4/C5 + §4.1)
  12. Why is decode ~6× faster than encode at the same resolution? (Capacity C7 + MM3 + Phase 8)
  13. Why does the hot macroblock loop fit in L2, and what would spill it? (Capacity C8 + §4.1)
  14. What is the worst-case output buffer size and which input produces it? (Capacity C10 + §4.1 + ADR-05)
  15. Why is H.264 chosen over HEVC/AV1 for this build, and what would change to target HEVC? (Comparative Architecture + Alternative Architectures + Deferred Features Register)
  16. How is Lagrangian RD optimization parameterized, and what goes wrong minimizing distortion alone? (MM10 + Phase 11 + ADR-15)
  17. Why is the codec single-threaded by default, and where is parallelism safe? (ADR-08 + §4.1 concurrency + Scalability Analysis)
  18. How does the no-panic-on-malformed-input guarantee get verified? (§4.1 + ADR-20 + I7)
  19. What distinguishes baseline from High profile here, and why is 8×8 intra deferred? (Deferred Features Register + §1 scope + Phase 3)
  20. How is bit-exact conformance tested without a GPL reference decoder? (ADR-23 + §4.1 PSNR floor + Testing & Verification Strategy)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Alt-A — Wavelet / subband codec (no block transform).

frame --> [ whole-frame discrete wavelet transform ] --> quantize subbands --> entropy code

Wins when: blocking artifacts must be avoided and resolution/quality scalability is first-class (cinema, archival). Embodied by: JPEG 2000, Dirac/VC-2. Rejected because: the target is the block-based H.264 hybrid model; wavelets lack the motion-compensated macroblock structure this project teaches and have far less hardware decode support.

Alt-B — All-intra (Motion-JPEG style, no temporal prediction).

each frame coded independently: intra predict --> transform --> quantize --> entropy

Wins when: frame-accurate editing, lowest latency, and error resilience dominate (studio mezzanine, capture). Embodied by: Motion JPEG, ProRes, DNxHD. Rejected because: discarding inter-frame prediction forfeits the bulk of video compression (MM3); temporal prediction is the core lesson of the codec.

Alt-C — Learned / neural codec (autoencoder + entropy model).

frame --> [ neural encoder ] --> latent --> learned entropy model --> bitstream --> [ neural decoder ]

Wins when: maximum compression at any compute cost is the goal and a GPU is available (research, some streaming). Embodied by: DVC, neural-codec research stacks. Rejected because: it is non-deterministic across hardware, has no normative bitstream/conformance, and needs orders of magnitude more compute — incompatible with the bit-exact, CPU-only educational scope.

Alt-D — Next-generation block codec (HEVC/AV1).

frame --> quadtree/superblock partition (64×64+) --> CABAC/arithmetic --> in-loop filters (deblock+SAO/CDEF)

Wins when: ~50% better compression than H.264 justifies much higher encoder complexity (modern streaming). Embodied by: x265 (HEVC), SVT-AV1. Rejected (deferred) because: the quadtree, larger transforms, and mandatory arithmetic coding are a substantially larger project; H.264's fixed macroblock keeps the reconstruction loop and entropy roundtrip teachable.

Deferred Features Register

Every PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") anchor resolves here. Each row is out of the Phase-1–14 scope.

Feature Anchor Building Phase (if undeferred) Un-defer Trigger
8×8 luma intra (High profile) this Register row New intra-prediction modes in vc-intra Conformance against High-profile bitstreams is required
Weighted prediction (explicit) this Register row vc-inter extension Content with fades/flashes needs weighted P/B prediction
Full CABAC context tables (Annex D) this Register row vc-entropy CABAC completion Maximum H.264 compression efficiency is needed beyond simplified CABAC
Slice-parallel threading this Register row rayon slice partition (ADR-08) Real-time encode at higher resolutions requires intra-frame parallelism
Container mux/demux (MP4/MKV) this Register row New vc-container crate Output must interoperate with standard players/streaming
Hardware acceleration hooks this Register row VA-API/NVENC adapter Offloading to fixed-function hardware is required for throughput
Scene-cut detection + 2-pass VBR this Register row vc-ratectrl extension (ADR-16) Offline quality-optimized encoding with adaptive GOP is required

Glossary

Alphabetical. Each term is implementation-relevant; references to crates, types, phases, or invariants are explicit.

Term Definition
4:2:0 Chroma subsampling format: one U and one V sample per 2x2 luma block. Frame carries three Planes: full-res Y, half-res U and V.
AVC Advanced Video Coding — alternate name for H.264, ISO/IEC 14496-10 / ITU-T H.264. The normative standard for this codec.
B-frame Bi-predictive frame (FrameType::B). Each macroblock may reference one frame from each of two lists (L0, L1), averaged or weighted. Requires DPB reordering (Phase 11).
Binary arithmetic coding Core of CABAC: encodes each binary decision by partitioning the probability interval; renormalization emits bytes when the interval shrinks below a threshold (Phase 13).
Bit-exact decoding Invariant I1. Decoder output is uniquely determined by the bitstream using integer-only operations; no float, no platform-specific rounding.
BitReader / BitWriter MSB-first bit-level I/O types in vc-bitstream. Support exp-Golomb ue/se, byte-aligned reads, and emulation-prevention insertion/removal.
Block matching Motion estimation strategy: finds the reference block minimizing SAD/SATD cost. Full search or fast pattern (diamond/EPZS) from a predicted MV start (Phase 8).
Boundary strength (BS) Integer 0–4 per 4x4 edge, computed from MB type, MVs, coded flags. Controls deblocking filter aggressiveness (Phase 12, vc-deblock).
CABAC Context-Adaptive Binary Arithmetic Coding. Each syntax element is binarized and each bin coded with a context-selected probability; see Phase 13. Invariant I6.
CAVLC Context-Adaptive Variable-Length Coding. Encodes quantized residual coefficients as coeff_token + trailing_ones + levels + total_zeros + run_before VLC tables; Phase 7.
CBP (Coded Block Pattern) Bitmask in Macroblock.cbp indicating which 4x4/8x8 transform blocks contain nonzero coefficients; avoids transmitting all-zero blocks.
Chroma Color-difference components U (Cb) and V (Cr) after YUV conversion. In 4:2:0 they are subsampled 2x in each dimension relative to luma.
Context model CABAC state: a probability estimate (MPS + state index) associated with a syntax element bin. Updated after each coded symbol via a lookup table.
DCT (integer) H.264-style separable 4x4 (or 8x8) transform implemented with integer butterfly operations. Exactly invertible at defined precision; no floating point. See Phase 3, vc-transform, P3, I3.
Dead-zone Extended quantization interval around zero in vc-quant. Coefficients inside the dead-zone are forced to zero, improving coding efficiency at the cost of slightly greater distortion.
Decoded Picture Buffer (DPB) Dpb type. Stores reference frames for inter prediction. Manages marking (short-term/long-term/unused), list construction, and POC-based reordering. I8, Phase 11.
Deblocking filter In-loop filter in vc-deblock applied to 4x4 block edges after reconstruction. Reduces blocking artifacts; must be applied identically in encoder and decoder (P6, I9, Phase 12).
Drift Divergence between encoder reconstructed frames and decoder frames accumulating over a GOP. Prevented by P2 (drift-free encoder reconstruction loop) and validated by I2.
EPZS Enhanced Predictive Zonal Search — fast motion estimation pattern starting from predicted MV, checking spatial/temporal candidates, then expanding diamond patterns. Phase 8.
Emulation prevention 0x03 byte inserted by NAL framing when output would contain 0x000001 (start code). vc-bitstream inserts on write and removes on parse. I7.
Exp-Golomb Variable-length code family (ue: unsigned, se: signed) used in slice/MB syntax. Code: prefix (unary) + suffix (binary) for value. Implemented in BitWriter/BitReader.
Frame Frame type: three Planes (Y, U, V), POC, FrameType. The unit of encoding/decoding.
GOP Group of Pictures: an IDR I-frame followed by a cadence of P- and B-frames. Defines the reference structure and key-frame interval. Phase 11.
H.264 ITU-T H.264 / ISO-IEC 14496-10 AVC. The normative standard governing all syntax, transform, prediction, and entropy coding rules implemented here.
Hadamard transform Integer transform applied to the 4x4 grid of luma DC coefficients in 16x16 intra mode, and to the 2x2 chroma DC. Improves coding of low-frequency DC energy. vc-transform.
I-frame Intra-coded picture (FrameType::I). All macroblocks use intra prediction from decoded neighbors within the same frame. The first frame of a GOP.
IDR Instantaneous Decoder Refresh. An I-frame that also clears the DPB; no subsequent frame references anything before the IDR.
In-loop filter See deblocking filter. "In-loop" means it runs inside the prediction loop, modifying reference frames; distinct from a post-processing filter.
Integer DCT See DCT (integer).
Inter prediction Prediction of a macroblock from a previously decoded reference frame using motion-compensated block copy. Produces a residual smaller than the original block.
Intra prediction Prediction of a macroblock from decoded spatial neighbors (left/top pixels) within the current frame. 4x4 (9 modes), 16x16 (4 modes), 8x8 chroma. P4, Phase 5.
Lambda Rate-distortion trade-off constant. RD cost = distortion + lambda * bits. Calibrated to QP. Used for mode decision (P5, Phase 13).
Luma The Y component of YUV, representing luminance (brightness). Full resolution in 4:2:0.
Macroblock Macroblock type. 16x16 luma + 8x8 U + 8x8 V region. The basic coding unit: carries MB type, QP, partitions, residual blocks, CBP.
Motion compensation Fetch of a prediction block from a reference frame at a sub-pixel position indicated by a MotionVector. Implemented in vc-inter.
Motion estimation Search for the MV minimizing prediction error (SAD/SATD). Implemented in vc-motion with full/diamond/EPZS strategies.
Motion vector MotionVector { x: i16, y: i16 } in quarter-pel units. Signed displacement from the current macroblock position to the reference block.
MVD Motion Vector Difference: MV minus the median prediction of neighboring MVs. Only MVD is coded in the bitstream; the predictor is re-derived at the decoder.
NAL unit NalUnit type. Framing unit: a 1-byte header (nal_type) + RBSP payload. Units are delimited by 0x000001 start codes in Annex B format.
P-frame Predictive picture (FrameType::P). Macroblocks use inter prediction from L0 references or intra prediction. Phase 10.
Partition Partition type: sub-macroblock division shape (16x16 down to 4x4) with its own MV and reference index.
Plane Plane { data, width, height, stride }. One component buffer. Stride >= width allows padding/alignment.
POC Picture Order Count. Display order index for a frame, used for DPB reference list construction and B-frame bi-prediction ordering.
PSNR Peak Signal-to-Noise Ratio in dB. Quality metric: 10*log10(255^2 / MSE). Used to validate the RD curve and invariant I10.
QP Qp(u8) in 0..51. Quantization parameter controlling step size via H.264's multiply-shift tables. Higher QP = coarser quantization = lower quality + fewer bits.
Quarter-pel Sub-pixel precision of motion vectors (1/4 luma pixel). Half-pel via 6-tap H.264 filter; quarter-pel via bilinear averaging of half-pel positions. vc-inter, Phase 9.
RBSP Raw Byte Sequence Payload. The logical payload of a NAL unit after emulation-prevention bytes are removed.
Rate control Algorithm in vc-encoder adjusting QP per-frame to match a target bitrate. Basic implementation in Phase 13.
Reconstruction loop Encoder path: dequant -> inverse DCT -> add prediction -> deblock -> store in DPB. Must match decoder exactly (P2, I2).
Residual source block - prediction block. The signal compressed by transform + quant + entropy coding. Separation principle: P9.
SAD Sum of Absolute Differences. Block-matching distortion metric; integer, fast. Used for motion estimation in vc-motion.
SATD Sum of Absolute Transformed Differences. Hadamard-transformed SAD; better rate-distortion proxy than SAD for mode decisions.
Slice A consecutive sequence of macroblocks parsed with a shared SliceHeader. Carries slice_type (I/P/B), frame_num, POC, reference list, initial QP.
Stride Row pitch of a Plane in bytes. Stride >= width; may be rounded up for alignment. Address of sample (x,y) = data[y*stride + x].
YUV Luma-chroma color representation. Y = luminance, U (Cb) and V (Cr) = color difference. Converted from RGB via BT.601/BT.709 matrices. vc-color.

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Cumulative duration assumes a solo experienced engineer working at pace. Phase durations sum to approximately 28 weeks total.

Phase Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria
1 vc-core types + vc-color RGB<->YUV 4:2:0 1 week BT.601 vs BT.709 rounding discrepancy; stride alignment bugs Color roundtrip property test passes; raw YUV read/write verified
2 vc-bitstream BitWriter/BitReader, exp-Golomb, NAL framing 1 week Emulation-prevention corner cases (consecutive 0x00 bytes) parse(write(syntax)) == syntax for all syntax elements; stuffing roundtrip correct
3 vc-transform integer 4x4/8x8 DCT + Hadamard 1 week Overflow in integer butterfly accumulation; precision loss vs spec idct(dct(block)) == block bit-exact for all test vectors
4 vc-quant QP ladder, dead-zone, dequant 0.5 weeks Chroma QP offset miscalculation; signed coefficient sign preservation `
5 vc-intra 4x4 (9 modes), 16x16, 8x8 chroma 1.5 weeks Availability checks wrong at slice/frame borders; DC fallback not triggered All modes match known-vector outputs; edge/corner cases correct
6 Intra-only end-to-end (I-frame codec) 1.5 weeks Reconstruction loop mismatch causing visible artifacts; placeholder entropy errors Decoder reconstructs encoder output bit-exactly; PSNR > 35 dB at mid QP
7 vc-entropy CAVLC encode + decode 2 weeks VLC table indexing off-by-one; context (nC) computation wrong cavlc_decode(cavlc_encode(coeffs)) == coeffs for all residual patterns
8 vc-motion integer-pel motion estimation 1.5 weeks EPZS candidate order affects quality; search window boundary handling Diamond search matches or improves on full search PSNR on test sequences
9 vc-inter half-pel and quarter-pel interpolation + MC 1.5 weeks 6-tap filter overflow; edge extension off by one; chroma 1/8-pel formula Known sub-pixel positions match reference; edge-extended fetches correct
10 P-frame inter prediction end-to-end 2 weeks MVD coding mismatch; skip MB reconstruction incorrect; CBP bugs P-frame encoder/decoder match bit-exactly; no drift over 16-frame sequence
11 B-frames, GOP structure, DPB management 2.5 weeks POC reordering subtle; L0/L1 list construction wrong; weighted prediction numerical issues 32-frame IDR+P+B GOP decodes with zero drift; PSNR monotone in QP
12 vc-deblock in-loop deblocking filter 1.5 weeks BS computation diverges between encoder and decoder; filter applied outside loop Filtered encoder reference == filtered decoder reference; blocking artifacts reduced visually
13 CABAC + RD mode decision + rate control + drivers 3 weeks CABAC renormalization precision; lambda calibration; rate control instability CABAC roundtrip exact; RD curve better than CAVLC-only baseline; bitrate within 15% of target
14 Hardening, conformance, fuzz, benchmarks, packing 2 weeks Fuzz finds parser panics; encoder recon drift discovered only at long GOP Zero decode panics on fuzz corpus; encode recon == decode output over 100-frame sequence; throughput > 10 fps at CIF
— Full build complete ~28 weeks total Accumulated technical debt in edge cases across all layers All tests pass; PSNR-vs-bitrate curve sane; ✅ tag earned (future coding phase)
